# Maple mobile UX friction audit (390x844, one thumb)

Audit date: 2026-08-28. Build audited: commit `f78bff5` (prod `budgeting-web-app-ruby.vercel.app` for J0-J2; the same commit served locally with Plaid sandbox for J1b-J17 because prod runs Plaid production and cannot link a sandbox bank).
Primary user: the household owner on an iPhone, whose bank feeds transactions and balances through Plaid, whose daily job is to categorize incoming transactions in place and sometimes split out GST, and who splits shared expenses with other members.
Method: every journey below was played live as that user with one goal at a time; every screen also got a code-reading pass, and each finding says whether it was seen live, read in code, or both.
Screenshots referenced below live in `../ux-audit/shots/<journey>/` (the folder beside this repository); the merged findings are in `ux-audit-mobile-2026-08-28.findings.json` next to this file.

## Part 0 - Read this first

### The verdict against the product model

The product model is: the bank feeds transactions and balances, the user's whole daily job is to categorize what arrived (and sometimes split out GST), and nobody should ever need a review mode or type a balance.
Measured against that model, the app today makes the daily job start with a guess, hides the result of the one action the user does most, and shows the wrong number for every linked account.

- From a cold open with 19 uncategorized transactions sitting in the household, the dashboard says nothing about them: no count, no card, no badge (J3, live).
- The pile lives only on the Transactions tab, only for the month in view, and its pill counts "need a category or title", so 15 uncategorized July rows were invisible and already-categorized rows with cryptic bank titles were counted (J3, live; `transactions/page.tsx:201`, `lib/title.ts:141`).
- Categorizing in place is the right shape (tap Categorize, tap a chip, no navigation) but in 4 of 6 attempts the row snapped back to "Uncategorized" with no error although the server had saved it; the change only appeared after a reload (J3, J7, live; `quick-categorize.tsx:43`, no optimistic state).
- Every one of the 14 linked accounts shows "$0.00 Opening" on /accounts while the header tiles and the dashboard cards show the real balances (J1b, J15, live; `accounts/row.tsx:172-179`).
- The balance sheet's primary button invites the user to type a balance for every linked account, and the goal form asks for typed "current progress" even when a funding account is linked (J12, live; `balance-sheet/form.tsx:126`, `goals/add-form.tsx:54`).
- The reported bank re-auth bug reproduces exactly: after `ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED` nothing on /dashboard, /accounts or /transactions changes; "Sync now" answers "No connected banks to sync." with the bank listed one line above; a completed re-auth prints "Re-authenticated. Syncing…" and the pill still says "Re-auth needed" after reload (J14, live).
  Root cause: `plaid-setup/actions.ts:426-433` filters `.in('status', ['active','error'])` even when a specific item id is passed, so the post-re-auth sync matches nothing, returns an error that `plaid-connect.tsx:424-430` discards, and status never returns to `active`.

### The three headline metrics

| Metric | Current | Spec target | Proposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Taps from open to one categorized incoming transaction | 3 minimum (Transactions tab, Categorize, chip), 6 realistic (category not in the six chips: More…, picker, option, Apply); 4 of 6 gave no visible result | 3 or fewer | 2 (dashboard inbox card or tab, then a chip that is already visible on the row; type-ahead for the long tail; optimistic update) |
| Taps to log a manual cash purchase (secondary path) | 6 minimum, 8 realistic from /dashboard; from /budgets or /accounts the "+" leaves the screen (route change, 3 s wait, 1 tap back) | 3 or fewer | 4 (sheet opens in place everywhere, account = last used, recent categories first, Save pinned above the keyboard) |
| Taps to split out GST | 6 plus two mental calculations (5% and the remainder); the "Balanced" badge was wrong mid-entry and both category selects reset to "Uncategorized" after saving | n/a | 2 (Splits, then "Split out GST 5%") |
| Overview answers "how much can I still spend on food" with zero taps and zero scrolls | No: the first widget mentioning a category sits at y = 931 px (below the 844 px fold) and shows spend without budget; the only budget widget is opt-in and household-total only | Yes | Yes: per-category "left of" list in the default layout, overspent first |
| Overview answers "is anything waiting for me" with zero taps and zero scrolls | No: zero mentions of uncategorized on the dashboard | Yes | Yes: "19 to categorize" card directly under the greeting |
| Primary actions outside the thumb zone or behind the keyboard at the moment they are needed | 10 measured live: Add transaction Save (behind the decimal keypad), Save splits (y 500-544 at keyboard-open height), Save budgets bar (not sticky, y = 2383 at scroll 0), Add account (under the tab bar at keyboard height), Invite (under the tab bar), Create household (y 824-872, keyboard top 508), Connect a bank (y 1206 on empty /accounts; y 530 under the keyboard in onboarding), Record payment (four screens down a 3775 px page), Sign out (y 1015 inside the More sheet), Settings gear (top-right on every screen) | 0 | 0: Sheet footer slot plus visualViewport sizing, fix the sticky bar, pad `main` above the tab bar, move Record payment next to the owes line, move Sign out and Settings into the More sheet's first row |

### Ranked top 10 changes (impact on the daily job first; effort S/M/L)

1. [P1, M] Make in-place categorization show its result instantly and reliably: swap the "Uncategorized" badge for the chosen category optimistically on chip tap (`useOptimistic` in `transactions/row.tsx` / `quick-categorize.tsx:43`), collapse the strip, and trim `revalidate()` in `transactions/actions.ts:26` to the current route plus /dashboard and /budgets. Seen live in J3 and J7 (server 200, row unchanged until reload).
2. [P1, M] Put the pile where the user opens the app and count it household-wide: a "N to categorize · $X" card in `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` right after the greeting (`dashboard/client.tsx:48`, query in `dashboard/page.tsx`), a pill on /transactions that counts all months ("5 uncategorized · 15 from July", `transactions/page.tsx:90,196`), and drop `looksCryptic` from the queue so a title is never required (`transactions/page.tsx:201`, pill copy at `uncategorized-review.tsx:62`).
3. [P1, M] Fix the pending-state stalls: six different forms sat on a disabled "Saving…" / "Adding…" for 10 s to over 400 s after the server had already returned 200 (categories add on prod, Link & sync 40 s, rule creation 58 s, add transaction 48 s and 18 s, loan terms 38 s, record payment 10 s or more; J2, J1b, J3, J8, J12, J10). The service worker is not the cause (`public/sw.js:77` ignores non-GET). Cheapest discriminating checks: watch the server log for the revalidated routes' render time during an action (`transactions/actions.ts:26` revalidates six routes, /dashboard runs 13 queries with `force-dynamic`), retry with `experimental.viewTransition` off in `next.config.ts`, and retry with `revalidate()` reduced to one path. Until the cause is found, every form should resolve its own pending state from the action's return value and show an inline "Saved" or error, never wait for the full revalidation.
4. [P1, S + M] Bank re-auth: in `plaid-setup/actions.ts:426-433` apply the status filter only on the bulk path (no `itemRowId`), check the `triggerPlaidSync` result in `plaid-connect.tsx:424-430` before calling `onDone`, and change "No connected banks to sync." to "This bank needs re-authentication" when the item exists but is excluded (S). Then surface `login_required` on /dashboard and on the linked account rows with a one-tap "Reconnect" (M; today it is visible only on /setup and the plaid-setup page, `setup/bank-connections.tsx:29`, `plaid-wizard.tsx:199`).
5. [P2, S] Show the six category chips directly on every uncategorized row instead of behind a "Categorize" toggle (`transactions/row.tsx:251-264, 320-329`), keep "More…" and "+ New" as the last chips, and give "More…" a type-ahead list instead of the native picker (`category-select.tsx`). This turns the realistic path from 6 taps into 2.
6. [P1, S] Show the real balance on every account row: pass `accountBalanceAt(...)` (already computed at `accounts/page.tsx:77-82`) into `AccountRow` and replace `opening_balance_cents` and the "Opening" caption at `accounts/row.tsx:172-179`; add a bank badge and last-synced time to linked rows (`plaid_account_id` is not even selected at `accounts/page.tsx:37`). Then make linked rows read-only in the balance-sheet form (`balance-sheet/form.tsx:126`) and derive goal progress from the funding account (`goals/add-form.tsx:54`).
7. [P1, M] Make the overview answer the two daily questions: a per-category "left of budget" list (overspent first, capped at five) in the default dashboard layout (`dashboard/client.tsx:48, 654`, `dashboard/page.tsx:271` collapses per-category budgets to one total today), and stop labelling unbudgeted categories "Over by $X" in red on /budgets (J6, `budgets/table.tsx:317-323`).
8. [P1, M] Keep the primary button reachable while typing: add a footer slot use in `Sheet` (`components/ui/sheet.tsx:135`) and size the panel from `window.visualViewport` (`sheet.tsx:110`), move Add transaction / Save splits / Add account / Invite / Create household submits into that footer or above the keyboard, fix the budgets save bar (the `overflow-x: hidden` on `body` at `globals.css:361` turns body into the scroll container and defeats `sticky`), and pad `main` by the tab bar height so nothing sits under it (J9a, J1, J5).
9. [P1, M] GST split in two taps: add "Split out GST (5%)" next to "Add split" (`split-editor.tsx:151`) that computes the tax, reduces the first row and appends a Taxes row using a remembered tax category; commit `MoneyInput` on every valid keystroke so "Balanced" and "Apply remainder" are never stale (`money-input.tsx:104`, `split-editor.tsx:45`, J4); re-initialise the rows from the saved splits after "Save splits" so the selects do not reset (J4); make "Apply remainder" target the row that was not just edited (`split-editor.tsx:169`).
10. [P1, S] Fix the numbers that are simply wrong: Net on /transactions is `outflow - inflow` rendered unsigned and green (`transactions/page.tsx:154, 279`); liabilities show "$4,321.00" in red on the dashboard card but "-$4,321.00" on /accounts; the manual add-account form silently saved "12.505" as $0.00 (`accounts/actions.ts` parseForm) and the add-transaction amount strips a typed comma so "12,50" becomes $1,250.00 (`add-form.tsx:95`). One sign rule (explicit minus for money out and liabilities, plus for money in), one formatter, reject what cannot be parsed.

Quick wins outside the top 10 (all S): "Add goal" does nothing because the sheet closes itself on mount (`goals/add-form.tsx:18`, copy the `wasPending` guard from `goals/row.tsx:161`; J12, J16); persist "Hide balances" and offer it on every money screen (`dashboard/client.tsx:147`; J15); Enter confirms a destructive modal even when focus is on Cancel (`confirm-button.tsx:174`); Categories are reachable only 1540 px down the Setup page (add a More-sheet tile; J2); "Skip for now" on the onboarding bank step ends onboarding instead of going to step 3 (`onboarding/bank/page.tsx`; J0); two identical Uber rules can be created and a paused rule looks active (J7, J16); the offline page has no navigation (J17); the "Set up sync" action on /transactions leads to the Gmail tutorial even when a bank is linked (`transactions/page.tsx`, J7); an invite failure shows the email provider's raw error text (J9a); the iOS install hint is fixed over the content and covers whichever transaction row scrolls under it (J9b, `components/pwa/ios-install-hint.tsx`); the Clear split button is 38 px wide (J9c); the plaid-setup page prints environment variable names to users (`plaid-setup/page.tsx:71-100`).

### What already works and should be copied

- Splits expand in place under the row with no modal; that is the shape categorization should keep (J4).
- "Record a payment by hand" opens already filled in: from Audit Partner, to Playwright, $42.11 with "Outstanding: $42.11" under it, today's date; nothing to type, only confirm (J10). Every form that can know its answer should arrive like this.
- The invite landing page tells a new member what the household will and will not see of their money before they sign in, and the sign-in carries `next=` back to the invite so it is never lost (J9b).
- Sharing a row is one tap with an immediate "SHARED" badge and a toast (J9c); the same immediacy is what categorization lacks.
- The Filter sheet pins "Clear all / Show results" in a footer with 44 px rows (`components/ui/filter-sheet.tsx`); every form sheet should use the same footer for its primary action.
- Category rename updates the row instantly on the client (J16); that is the optimistic pattern the rest of the app lacks.
- The CSV import result line says exactly what happened ("Imported 6 transactions." then "Imported 0 transactions, enriched 6 existing.") and re-import never duplicates (J13); pull-to-refresh and "Sync now" should report in the same words.
- Archive and delete confirmations name what is lost and offer the right secondary choice ("Also unshare the 6 transactions this rule shared", J16).
- Offline: the banner appears, the add-transaction sheet still opens, and a save made offline landed within 10 s of reconnecting (J5, J17).
- Tap targets are 44 px tall almost everywhere and inputs are 16 px, so iOS never zooms (measured across screens); Lighthouse mobile accessibility scored 100 on /dashboard and 96 on /transactions (J17).

### How to read the rest

Part 1 is the user's first weeks, journey by journey, in the first person, with every friction point and the screenshot that shows it.
Part 2 is every screen, sheet and state in the spec's block format, with each finding marked `[seen live: J..]` when a journey hit it or `[code]` when only the code shows it.
Part 3 holds the cross-screen inconsistencies, the flows to shorten, what to delete, what to move into the thumb zone, the score table, the journey table and the reported-bug trace.
Environment: J0, J1 and J2 ran on production; production runs Plaid in its production environment, so the sandbox bank cannot be linked there and J1b through J17 ran against a local `next start` of the same commit with `PLAID_ENV=sandbox` and the same database.

## Part 1 - A user's first weeks with the app (journeys)

### J0 - Day 1 evening, brand-new owner - "Set the app up so our bank feeds it."

Goal reached: NO. Taps 12, keystrokes 75, scrolls 3, decisions 6, route changes 7, return taps 4.
Key numbers: inputs_before_first_useful_screen = 2; required_steps_that_could_not_be_skipped = ["household (name + display name)"]; steps_silently_skipped_by_Skip_for_now = ["invite","budget"]; taps_cold_start_to_new_dashboard = 11; route_changes_to_new_dashboard = 4; connect_a_bank_tap_to_plaid_visible_ms = 4000; plaid_phone_screen_to_bank_list_ms = 2500; sign_out_confirmation = none; sign_out_button_y_in_more_sheet = 1015; create_household_button_bottom_px = 872; keyboard_top_px = 508; bank_step_connect_button_y = 530; bank_step_active_element_on_load = input[name=name] (manual form); skip_for_now_y = 1031; accounts_primary_connect_y = 1206; dashboard_add_one_link_px = 52x17; dashboard_text_under_16px_pct = 80; plaid_token_env = link-production-*; imported_transactions_on_dashboard = 0; needs_category_message = absent.

I start on the E2E dashboard and need to get out first.
More is the last tab; the sheet that slides up is a wall of tiles and Sign out is not in it until I drag the sheet up about 240px - it is a plain grey text link in the bottom-left corner next to Reset tabs.
I tap it and I am out instantly, no 'are you sure' - fine for a sign-out, but the page flashes '/' for a second before /sign-in appears.
Signing in as the new owner drops me straight into 'Step 1 of 4 - Welcome.
Let's set up your household.' The headline block is huge (40px serif plus a paragraph) so the form starts 560px down; the household field is already focused, which I like, but the Create household button is off-screen at y=824 and would be under the keyboard.
Both names are required; the little preview card updates as I type, and Enter submits, so I never had to hunt for the button.
Step 2, 'Where does your money live?', is where my goal lives.
There is a green Connect a bank button - but the cursor is blinking in the manual 'Name' field below it.
On my phone the keyboard would have shot up over the Connect button before I read anything; the app is telling me typing an account is the normal thing to do.
I tap Connect a bank; it says 'Working...' instantly and about four seconds later Plaid's card slides up asking for a US +1 phone number.
I did not expect that in a Canadian app; the way out is a low-contrast 'Continue without phone number' link under a disabled Continue.
Then a proper list of Canadian banks.
I search my bank, it is not there (the test bank does not exist on this production Plaid key), so I hit X; Plaid asks 'Are you sure?' and I say yes.
Back on step 2 nothing acknowledges what just happened - no 'no bank connected, try again or add by hand', and the manual Name field grabs focus again.
To move on I have to scroll past the whole manual form to find 'Skip for now' at y=1031.
I tap it expecting step 3 of 4 - instead I am on the dashboard: the invite and budget steps never happened and nobody told me.
The dashboard greets me 'Bonjour, Audit.' over a big dark $0.00 net-worth card with a flat line and a 1Y selector, three $0.00 tiles (Spent is in red), and two dashed boxes: 'No accounts yet.
Add one.' and 'No categorised expenses this month.
Add some.' Nothing says connect your bank, nothing says N transactions need a category, and the only affordances are 14px underlined words.
I tap Add one; /accounts opens with a small outlined Connect a bank up top, then a full manual Add account form (Name, Type, Ownership, Opening balance, Last 4 digits) and only below all of that, at y=1206, the big green Connect a bank.
I never got the bank feeding the app; I got two forms asking me to type balances.
I sign out (same buried link) and back in as the E2E owner and land on /dashboard.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Manual account 'Name' input (FirstAccountForm) | The manual Name field is auto-focused, so on a phone the keyboard opens the moment step 2 appears and covers the green Connect a bank button (button y=530-576, keyboard top 508). The app visually tells me that typing an account is the expected path, and Plaid is what you reach after dismissing the keyboard. | The product model says bank linking is the default and manual entry is the exception; the first screen of the bank step pushes the exception into my hands and hides the primary action. | Remove `autoFocus` from the Name input in src/app/onboarding/bank/form.tsx. Do not focus anything on this step; if focus management is wanted, move focus to the 'Connect a bank' button. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/onboarding/bank/form.tsx:46) (screenshot: 06-step2-bank.png)
- [P1] 'Skip for now' footer button (StepFooter skip="finish") | The header says 'Step 2 of 4'. I tap 'Skip for now' expecting step 3 (invite). Instead onboarding ends and I am on the dashboard; the invite and budget steps never appear and nothing tells me they were skipped. Skip also sits at y=1031, below the entire manual form. | The label and the step counter promise a next step; the button actually abandons the guided flow. A user who could not connect a bank tonight loses the rest of the setup and the dashboard gives no way back to it. | In src/app/onboarding/bank/page.tsx pass `skip={{ href: '/onboarding/invite' }}` so Skip advances to step 3; keep the 'finish' behaviour only on the last step. If ending the flow here is intended, rename the button to 'Skip setup, go to my dashboard' and render it directly under the Connect a bank card (above the manual form) rather than after it. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/onboarding/bank/page.tsx:68, budgeting-web-app/src/app/onboarding/step-footer.tsx:42) (screenshot: 12-bank-step-scrolled-skip.png)
- [P1] Dashboard for a household with zero accounts (net-worth hero + Accounts empty state) | The first thing I see is a dark $0.00 Net worth card with a flat line and 1M/3M/YTD/1Y/ALL buttons, three $0.00 tiles, and then below the fold 'No accounts yet. Add one.' Nothing says connect your bank; nothing says how many transactions need a category. The only next-step affordances are 14px underlined words 'Add one' (52x17 px) and 'Add some' (64x17 px). | A first-run dashboard should hand the user their one job. Here the primary product promise (bank feeds the app) is invisible and the only links are sub-44px inline text pointing at manual pages. | In src/app/(app)/dashboard/client.tsx, when accounts.length === 0 replace the net-worth hero with a full-width 'Connect your bank' card: one primary button that opens Plaid Link in place (reuse PlaidConnect variant='plain'), a secondary text button 'Add an account by hand' to /accounts. When accounts exist and uncategorised transactions > 0, render a strip 'N transactions need a category' above the hero linking to /transactions (uncategorised filter). Make the empty-state links 44px-high block buttons. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/dashboard/client.tsx:456) (screenshot: 13-dashboard-after-skip.png)
- [P2] Page order: Add account card before the empty-state Connect a bank | Following 'Add one' I land on a page whose first big block is a manual form (Name, Type, Ownership, Opening balance, Last 4 digits). The primary green Connect a bank is at y=1206, after ~700px of form; the only above-fold Connect a bank is a small outlined secondary button in the header. | Again the manual exception is laid out as the default. Two 'Connect a bank' buttons with different styles on one page also read as two different actions. | In src/app/(app)/accounts/page.tsx, when visible.length === 0 render the EmptyState with the primary Connect a bank first and collapse the Add account card behind a 'Add an account by hand' disclosure (details/summary or a toggle). When accounts exist keep the current order but drop the header duplicate. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/accounts/page.tsx:143, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/accounts/page.tsx:164) (screenshot: 15-accounts-empty.png)
- [P2] PlaidConnect onExit | After 'Yes, exit' the Plaid card vanishes and step 2 looks exactly as before: no toast, no 'No bank connected yet - try again or add by hand', and focus jumps back into the manual Name field. | A silent cancel leaves me guessing whether something was saved. The natural recovery (try another institution, or come back later) is not offered. | In src/components/plaid/plaid-connect.tsx onExit (line 129 and 433) call the toast helper with 'No bank connected. Try again any time from Accounts.' and, on the onboarding variant, show an inline note under the button with a 'Try again' action. Do not re-focus the manual form. (budgeting-web-app/src/components/plaid/plaid-connect.tsx:129) (screenshot: 11-after-plaid-exit.png)
- [P2] Plaid phone-number screen | Before I can pick a bank Plaid asks for a US-flag +1 phone number. The Continue button is disabled and the way past it is a small text link 'Continue without phone number'. Extra decision, extra tap, and it looks American. | One more screen between me and the bank list, and the +1 flag makes a Canadian user hesitate. | Disable the Plaid 'returning user / phone number' Link experience for this client in the Plaid dashboard (Link > Customization), or set the link token so Layer/returning-user is off; the Link flow should open straight on 'Select your institution'. (screenshot: 07-plaid-link-open.png)
- [P2] Household step form + Create household button | The hero (eyebrow, 40px 3-line title, paragraph) pushes the first input to y=560 and the Create household button to y=824-872, entirely under a 336px keyboard. Enter does submit, which saved me, but there is no enterkeyhint so the keyboard shows 'return', and moving between the two fields needs a tap. | On the very first screen the primary button is not reachable while typing; users who do not know Enter submits will close the keyboard and scroll. | On mobile shrink the hero (title 32px, drop the intro paragraph to a 13px footnote) so the form starts above y=300; add enterKeyHint="next" on household_name and enterKeyHint="done" on member_name; keep Enter submitting. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/onboarding/form.tsx:50) (screenshot: 04-after-signin-owner.png)
- [P3] Required 'Your display name' field | Two mandatory text inputs before anything useful. I typed my name a second time even though the account already knows my email. | Every required field before the bank step is a drop-off point; the household name could default from the person's name. | Default household_name to '<display name>'s household' as the user types their name (still editable), and prefill member_name from the sign-up name if one is collected there. Keep both editable, neither blank. (screenshot: 05-household-filled.png)
- [P3] '0 of 10 banks linked' counter | A first-timer sees a quota counter before they have linked anything; it reads like a limit warning. | Noise on the most important step. | Hide the counter until linkedCount >= 1 (render it as 'RBC connected - 1 of 10' style only after a link). (budgeting-web-app/src/app/onboarding/bank/page.tsx:82) (screenshot: 06-step2-bank.png)
- [P3] Sign out button | Sign out is a 14px grey text button that only appears after scrolling the More sheet ~240px, sitting next to 'Reset tabs'. It fires immediately with no confirmation (acceptable for sign-out), then the page shows '/' for about a second before /sign-in. | Not dangerous, but it is hard to find and the double redirect looks like a glitch. | Keep no-confirm. Make the signOut server action redirect straight to /sign-in instead of '/', and render the Sign out row as a full-width 44px list row directly under the email line at the top of the More sheet. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:693) (screenshot: 02-more-sheet-scrolled-signout.png)
- [P3] 'Spent $0.00' stat tile | Spent is painted red at $0.00 while Income and Saved are green; with nothing spent, red reads as a warning. | Colour carries meaning with no sign or word to back it; at zero it is simply wrong-feeling. | Use the neutral ink colour for any tile whose value is 0, and only apply the maple tone when spent > 0. (screenshot: 13-dashboard-after-skip.png)

Could not test: Plaid connection and the account-mapping wizard: the deployment issues link-production tokens and searching 'Tartan', 'Tartan Bank', 'Platypus', 'Houndstooth' returns nothing; needs PLAID_ENV=sandbox on a preview deployment (or a sandbox-flagged household) to test mapping decisions, defaults and the imported-transactions dashboard state. | Onboarding steps 3 (invite) and 4 (budget): never reached because 'Skip for now' on the bank step ends the flow; they can only be seen after adding at least one account. | Dashboard 'N transactions need a category' state: cannot be observed without imported transactions. | Whether the service-worker first-install reload resets an in-flight Plaid Link session (I pre-empted it with the prescribed wait + reload). | /transactions/import/plaid-setup (target of the Accounts 'Connect a bank' buttons) was not visited.

### J1 - Day 1, owner - "Link the bank so accounts and balances come in on their own."

Goal reached: NO. Taps 41, keystrokes 135, scrolls 6, decisions 11, route changes 4, return taps 3.
Key numbers: taps_dashboard_to_plaid_iframe = 4; route_changes_to_plaid = 2; connect_tap_to_iframe_ms = 854; plaid_phone_to_bank_list_ms = 2570; plaid_env = production (link-production-*); tartan_search_result = No results found; I open the dashboard cold and it is the same empty picture J0 saw: a dark $0.00 Net worth card, three $0.00 tiles, and below the fold 'No accounts yet.
Add one.' Nothing says 'connect your bank'.
I try More first because that is where apps hide setup; the sheet has Accounts, Rules, reports, Goals, Setup - no bank entry.
I tap Accounts.
The accounts page opens on a paragraph about ledgers, then a small outlined 'Connect a bank' next to 'Archived (0)', then the You have / You owe / Net worth tiles, then a 700px manual form.
I tap Connect a bank and land on a page called 'Bank sync' whose headline is 'Link your bank.
Get real merchant names.' with copy about email-alert rows and '0 of 10 banks linked' - I have not linked one yet and it is already counting.
A second green Connect a bank button, tap four, opens Plaid in about 0.9 s.
Plaid asks for a US +1 phone number first; I use the small 'Continue without phone number' link.
The institution list is RBC, Scotiabank, TD, BMO, CIBC, Tangerine - real banks.
I search Tartan: 'No results found'.
The link token in the network log is link-production-*, so this deployment talks to production Plaid and there is no sandbox institution to connect.
I cannot put real bank credentials into an audit, so the primary path ends here: I tap X, 'Are you sure?', 'Yes, exit', and the page behind looks exactly as before - no toast, no 'nothing was connected', focus dumped on body, and the Plaid iframe is still in the DOM, hidden.
So I fall back to the exception path: More, Accounts again.
The form asks Name, Type, Ownership, Opening balance (CAD) and Last 4 digits - four of those five Plaid would have known.
The balance field is pre-filled '0.00' with the caret at the end, so every account starts with four backspaces.
I probe the field: 1,234.56, 12.505, 1250 and 12,50 are all accepted verbatim, nothing reformats, nothing warns.
Then I add Audit Cash (123456.78), Audit Visa (4321.00), Audit Car Loan (18500.00) and Audit Old Account typed as 12.505.
Each Add takes 3-5 s with the button reading 'Adding...', no toast, the form wipes itself (Type snaps back to Chequing) and the new row appears below the fold at y about 1017, so I scroll to check every time.
Audit Old Account saved as $0.00 - the 12.505 was thrown away silently.
I fix it through Edit (3.1 s save).
The rows show the number labelled 'OPENING'; the header tiles say $123.5K / $22.8K / $100.6K while the rows say $123,456.78 and -$18,500.00, and the dashboard says $100,645.78 exactly.
On the dashboard the four accounts are 240px cards in a horizontal rail, 1.5 visible, liabilities red but without a minus, ownership shown as a 6px dot.
With the keyboard up (390x508) the Add account button sits under the tab bar: tapping its centre hits 'Budgets'.
I ended signed in as the owner on /dashboard with four manual accounts and no bank linked.

### J1b - Day 1, owner (local sandbox build) - "Link the bank so accounts and transactions come in on their own."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 18, keystrokes 24, scrolls 2, decisions 6, route changes 6, return taps 2.
Key numbers: taps_dashboard_to_plaid = 4; route_changes_dashboard_to_plaid_setup = 2; plaid_phone_default_country = CA +1 (Canada flag) - sandbox, differs from J1's production Plaid US flag; tartan_search_result = Tartan-Dominion Bank of Canada (1 result); plaid_accounts_offered = 14; plaid_accounts_selectable = false; link_to_first_rows_ms_approx = 41000; link_sync_button_stuck_state = Saving... with no spinner/toast for ~40s before resolving; transactions_imported_total_sync = 48; transactions_visible_this_month = 33; uncategorized_count_this_month = 19; sync_result_text_initial = OK · +48 new · 0 enriched; sync_result_text_manual_resync = OK · +0 new · 0 enriched; linked_accounts = 14; balance_shown_as_accounts_page = Opening ($0.00 for every linked account - wrong); balance_shown_as_dashboard_cards = correct live current balance, no label (e.g. Plaid Auto Loan $23,211.33); balance_shown_as_accounts_header_tiles = correct: You have $210K / You owe $185.2K / Net worth $24.8K; linked_vs_manual_visual_difference = same row layout/label as manual accounts (still says 'Opening'); only difference is the ····last4 suffix and 'Joint' vs 'Mine' - no Plaid badge, sync icon, or 'linked' indicator on the row itself; toast_count_link_and_sync = 0; toast_count_manual_sync_now = 0; money_probe_accounts_page_zero_rows = 14; plaid_type_mapping_errors = ["tfsa->taxable_investment","rrsp->taxable_investment","cd->chequing","money market->chequing","hsa->chequing","cash management->chequing"]; review_dialog_aria_label = Triage progress.

I opened the dashboard already signed in and looked for a way to connect my bank.
Nothing on the dashboard itself mentions a bank - no card, no banner - so I opened More and found Accounts as the only lead.
On /accounts there is a small outlined 'Connect a bank' button in the header, above a manual add-account form and the existing four accounts from earlier days.
Tapping it took me to a dedicated /transactions/import/plaid-setup page ('Bank sync') with its own big green 'Connect a bank' button - so it took two 'Connect a bank' taps and two page loads before Plaid Link even opened.
Inside Plaid, unlike the production environment prior journeys hit, sandbox let me actually pick a bank: I skipped the phone-number step, searched 'Tartan', picked Tartan-Dominion Bank of Canada, logged in with the sandbox user_good/pass_good, and got a list of 14 accounts (chequing, savings, CD, credit card, money market, TFSA, RRSP, student loan, mortgage, HSA, cash management, auto loan, HELOC, business credit card) - all pre-checked, no way to deselect any of them.
I shared the data, declined to save my login for next time, and landed back in Maple on a 'Match accounts' wizard: 14 rows, each with a map-as/type/owner triple, all defaulting to 'Create a new Maple account' with a guessed type and Owner set to Joint (not Mine, which is what the manual form defaults to).
I accepted the defaults and tapped 'Link & sync'.
This is where things got rough: the button flipped to 'Saving...' and just sat there - no spinner, no toast, nothing else on the page moved - for what felt like the better part of a minute before it finally resolved into '1 of 10 banks linked' plus a 'Recent syncs' entry reading 'OK · +48 new · 0 enriched'.
On /transactions, the imported rows are sitting right in the ordinary list next to my existing entries, with real merchant names (United Airlines, Starbucks, Uber, Loblaws...) and an inline 'Categorize' button - exactly the in-place workflow the product wants, no separate import screen.
But a banner at the very top - '19 need a category or title · Review' - opens a modal one-at-a-time triage dialog whose own progress bar is literally labelled 'Triage progress' in the code, which is the exact thing the model says nobody wants.
And on /accounts, every one of my 14 new accounts shows '$0.00 Opening', while the page's own header tiles and the dashboard's account cards show the real Plaid balances for the same accounts - the code computes the right number for the tiles and then throws it away before it reaches the row.
So: the bank linked, transactions and balances did arrive on their own in the aggregate, but the accounts list actively lies about what's in each account, and a built triage mode sits one tap away from every session.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Account row balance (AccountRow, every linked account) | All 14 newly-linked Plaid accounts show '$0.00 Opening' in the accounts list, while the same page's own header tiles (You have $210K / You owe $185.2K) and the dashboard's account cards (e.g. 'Plaid Auto Loan $23,211.33') show the correct live Plaid balance for the exact same accounts. | accounts/page.tsx computes the correct per-account current balance via accountBalanceAt() specifically so the headline tiles don't drift from reality (see its own comment), sums that into the header totals, then discards the per-account value - AccountRow is only ever passed the raw, never-updated opening_balance_cents (which is 0 for every Plaid account) and unconditionally labels it 'Opening'. A user who links a bank and looks at the accounts list - the page whose whole job is 'where the money lives' - sees every one of their real accounts as empty, directly contradicting 'balances arrive on their own.' | In accounts/page.tsx, store the accountBalanceAt() result per account (e.g. a Map<id, cents>) inside the existing loop instead of only summing it, and pass that value into AccountRow as the displayed balance (replacing opening_balance_cents in the display, not the edit form). In row.tsx, drop the hardcoded 'Opening' label for linked/derived accounts (or relabel to 'Current' / show both opening and current) so the row matches the header tiles and the dashboard. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/accounts/page.tsx:76, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/accounts/page.tsx:189)
- [P1] '19 need a category or title · Review' pill -> Categorize transactions dialog | Tapping the butter-colored pill at the very top of the transactions page opens a modal that steps through uncategorized transactions one at a time (progress '1 of 19', Save & next / Skip, a progress bar whose accessible name is literally 'Triage progress'). | The product model states the user never wants a separate review/triage step and that categorizing in place is the whole job. The component's own doc-comment calls itself 'a focused triage queue' - this is a purpose-built review-mode UI, prominently placed as the first thing under the month picker, competing with the in-place 'Categorize' button that already exists on every uncategorized row (and which is the correct pattern - see J1b-F5). | Retire the modal triage flow (uncategorized-review.tsx) or demote it to an optional, out-of-the-way affordance; keep the count as a passive badge if useful, but stop routing the primary CTA into a one-at-a-time review dialog. Rely on the inline 'Categorize' button already on each row in the normal list, which already matches the model. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/uncategorized-review.tsx:30, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/uncategorized-review.tsx:237)
- [P1] 'Link & sync' button in the Match accounts wizard | After accepting the 14-row mapping wizard's defaults and tapping 'Link & sync', the button read 'Saving...' with no spinner, no toast, and no other change on the page for roughly 40 seconds before the wizard finally collapsed into a linked/synced state. | Same pattern as J2-F1 (category add hanging on 'Adding...'): the underlying mutation appears to commit quickly server-side, but the client-side pending UI drags on far longer than the actual work with zero indication of progress. A real user staring at a static 'Saving...' button for the better part of a minute - on the single highest-value action in this whole journey - would reasonably conclude the app froze, and either back out (losing the mapping work) or tap again (risking a duplicate bank link). | Investigate the shared root cause with J2-F1 (React 19 Server Action / revalidation not resolving the pending state promptly for larger mutations). At minimum, add an incremental progress indicator ('Linking 14 accounts...', or per-account ticks) so 40+ seconds of silence doesn't read as a hang. (budgeting-web-app/src/components/plaid/plaid-connect.tsx:undefined)
- [P2] Account selection checkboxes (Plaid) + per-row map/type/owner selects (Match accounts wizard) | Plaid presented 14 accounts with every checkbox pre-checked and disabled - no way to exclude any single account before import. The subsequent Match accounts wizard then forces a decision across all 14 rows (map-as / type / owner) with no bulk action ('map all as new', 'don't import the rest'), and Owner defaults to 'Joint' on every row, unlike the manual Add Account form which defaults to 'Mine'. | For an institution that returns more accounts than a typical single-owner Canadian retail user has (registered plans, HSA, business card, mortgage, HELOC, auto loan all at once), the only way through is to individually inspect and decide on all 14 rows, and the Joint default means a solo user must manually flip every single one back to 'Mine' or their whole net worth silently becomes shared. | Add a 'Skip the rest' / 'Import all as new' bulk control to the Match accounts wizard, and default Owner to the household's most common existing choice (or 'Mine' to match the manual form) rather than always 'Joint'. (budgeting-web-app/src/components/plaid/plaid-connect.tsx:undefined)
- [P2] Bank-link success and manual 'Sync now' feedback | No toast fired for either the completion of the first bank link + 48-transaction sync, or a manual 'Sync now' re-sync. The only feedback is small in-page text ('Bank linked. Transactions are syncing in.' then 'Synced - 0 new, 0 enriched.') that a user could easily miss if not looking directly at that spot. | Matches the app-wide pattern from J1/J2 of missing toast confirmation, but here it's on the single highest-value success event in the app (a first bank connection actually working). | Fire the existing toast component on successful link/sync completion, not just for manual account CRUD actions.
- [P3] Newly-synced Plaid rows in the ordinary transaction list | Imported transactions land directly in the normal /transactions list, interleaved with existing rows, each showing a real merchant name (United Airlines, Starbucks, Uber, Loblaws, Netflix...) with an inline 'Categorize' button next to Splits/Edit/Share - no separate import/staging screen at all. | This is exactly the in-place categorization workflow the product model asks for, and it directly contradicts the parallel triage dialog in J1b-F2. Worth calling out explicitly as the reference pattern: this is what 'no review step' looks like when done right, and it should be the only entry point kept. | No code change needed here. Use this as the justification when retiring/demoting the modal triage dialog in J1b-F2 - don't build a second, competing review surface.
- [P3] Auto-suggested 'Type' select for registered/investment accounts | Plaid TFSA and Plaid RRSP both default to Type='Taxable investment' instead of the app's own dedicated 'TFSA' / 'RRSP' options (which exist in the same dropdown); Plaid CD, Money Market, HSA, and Cash Management all default to 'Chequing'. | plaidSubtypeToAccountType() only special-cases 'checking', 'savings', credit, and loan subtypes, collapsing every Plaid investment-type account (including tfsa/rrsp subtypes) into 'taxable_investment' and falling through everything else to 'chequing'. Per CLAUDE.md, RRSP/TFSA/FHSA contribution tracking is a core, CRA-rules-driven feature of this app; an account imported as 'Taxable investment' instead of 'TFSA' won't be tracked for contribution-room purposes even though the user did nothing wrong - they accepted the suggested default as instructed. | In plaidSubtypeToAccountType() (transactions/import/plaid-setup/actions.ts), branch on subtype 'tfsa' -> 'tfsa', 'rrsp'/'roth'/'ira' family -> 'rrsp' where applicable, and give cd/money market/hsa/cash management their own reasonable mappings instead of falling through to 'chequing'. ### J2 - Day 1, owner - "Put rough numbers on food and coffee, the rest later."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 20, keystrokes 40, scrolls 12, decisions 7, route changes 6, return taps 1.
Key numbers: taps_dashboard_to_category_form_focused = 3; scrolls_dashboard_to_category_form = 3; route_changes_dashboard_to_category_form = 1; setup_page_doc_height_px = 3544; setup_categories_card_doc_y = 1540; categories_in_more_sheet = false; categories_page_reachable_from_nav = false; add_category_ms_to_first_dom_change = [842,1005,987]; add_category_toast = none; add_category_hang = third add stuck on 'Adding...' > 398 s; POST 200 {ok:true}; resolved only by reload; new_row_offset_below_form_px = 1133; child_collapsed_by_default = true; parent_select_remembered = false; default_taxes_has_gst_child = false; budgets_first_paint_blank_ms = >3200 (content present by ~6500); budgets_rows = 14; unbudgeted_row_value = 0.00 (pre-filled text, caret at index 0 on tap); typed_400_into_0.00_result = 4000.00; row_height_change_on_first_digit_px = 44; keyboardFit_coffee_vs_save_kb336 = {"input_y":469,"input_bottom":513,"submit_y":637,"submit_bottom":681,"keyboard_top":508,"submit_covered":true,"input_visible":false}; save_bar_sticky_effective = false; save_bar_y_at_scroll0 = 2383; save_bar_cause = html,body{overflow-x:hidden} makes body a scroll container (globals.css:361); save_ms_to_saving = 934; save_ms_to_saved = 1586; saved_indicator = 12px '✓ Saved' inside bar, ~2.5 s, no toast; post_save_scroll_jump_px = 178; post_save_header = $0.00 of $400.00 · 1 of 12 categories budgeted (child $50 not counted); budget_inputs_after_save = '400' and '50' (unformatted until reload -> 400.00 / 50.00); duplicate_input_ids = 28 inputs / 14 ids; budgets_text_under_16px_pct = 82; scope_toggle_default = Every month (standing); scope_toggle_explanation_on_phone = title tooltip only; unsaved_change_guard = none; keys_realistic_phone_estimate = 48; keys_note = Adds 4 backspaces per pre-filled budget field; 40 keys as executed.

I open the dashboard and look for somewhere to make a Food category.
The tab bar says Home, Transactions, +, Budgets, More.
Nothing says Categories, so I tap More.
The sheet has Accounts, Shared expenses, Rules, three reports, Goals, Contributions, Loans, Time off, and, after scrolling the sheet, Setup.
I guess Setup.
The Setup page is 3544px tall; Categories is the fifth card, 1540px down, under Household, Members, Bank connections and Default split.
Two drags later I find a name field, a Parent select and Add.
I type Audit Food and tap Add: about 850 ms, the field clears and keeps focus, no toast, and the new row lands at the very bottom of the list 1227px below the form where I cannot see it.
I type Audit Coffee, pick 'under Audit Food' and Add.
The parent select snaps back to Top level.
When I scroll down, Audit Food just shows a little triangle; Coffee is collapsed inside it until I tap.
For GST I look at the defaults: Taxes exists but has no children and reads like income tax to me, so I add Audit GST under Taxes.
This time the button says Adding... and never comes back.
The field is already empty, there is no error, nothing appears under Taxes.
I wait six minutes.
The server actually saved it (the POST returned ok with the new row), but the page is stuck, so I reload and there it is.
On Budgets the page paints just the banner and 'Pull to sync' for over three seconds, then 14 rows, every one pre-filled $0.00 with Spent $0.00 / Left $0.00.
Tapping 'Pick a category' scrolls me to the top of the list; my rows are at the bottom, two more drags.
I tap the Audit Food field, the caret lands at the start of 0.00, I type 400 and the field says 4000.00.
I fix it, then Coffee.
As soon as an amount is non-zero an 'Every month' pill appears and the row grows 44px, so the Coffee field jumps under my thumb.
The 'Save budgets' bar is described in code as sticky above the tab bar, but it is not sticking: it sits after the last row, and with the keyboard up it is 130px under the keyboard.
I dismiss the keyboard, tap Save: 'Saving...' at 0.9 s, a 12px '✓ Saved' at 1.6 s that disappears at 2.5 s, no toast, and the page jumps 178px because a summary card replaced the empty state above.
The summary says '1 of 12 categories budgeted' and '$0.00 of $400.00'; my $50 coffee budget is not mentioned and I cannot tell whether it is inside the 400 or on top.
I tap 'Every month' on Coffee to see what it does: it turns into 'August only' with no explanation except a hover tooltip I cannot hover on a phone.
I toggle it back and leave.
The numbers are saved, so I got what I came for, but it took a reload, a wrong amount, and a lot of scrolling.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Add button in the AddCategory form | I tapped Add. The button turned to 'Adding...' after ~1 s, the name field emptied, and then nothing happened - no row under Taxes, no error, no toast - for over six minutes. The POST had already returned {ok:true} and the RSC payload in that response contained Audit GST under Taxes, but the client transition never settled. I had to reload to see that my category existed. Had the server failed instead, my typed name would be gone too, because the form is reset before the result is known. | A pending state that never resolves after a successful write means the user cannot tell whether to retry (creating a duplicate) or give up. The form clearing before the server answers loses the user's input on failure. | In categories-list.tsx AddCategory: keep the typed name in state and only clear it after res.ok; render the new category optimistically (append to a local list) instead of relying on the router refresh to repaint; add a 5 s watchdog that turns 'Adding...' back into 'Add' with an inline 'Saved - pull down if you do not see it' or 'Could not add, try again' message; log the transition outcome so the stuck case can be diagnosed. Also show a 'Added Audit GST under Taxes' toast on success. The pattern was already flagged for Opening balance in J1-F7; here it produces a wrong number rather than extra backspaces. | In table.tsx initialise amounts[id] to '' when budgetByCat[id] is 0 and give the input placeholder='0.00'; for rows that do have a value, select-all on focus (onFocus={(e) => e.target.select()}); on blur normalise the string with Number(...).toFixed(2); keep inputMode='decimal' and add enterKeyHint='done'. ### J3 - Day 2 morning, owner, coffee in hand - THE daily job - "The bank dropped in new transactions. Categorize them."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 32, keystrokes 15, scrolls 4, decisions 12, route changes 6, return taps 2.
Key numbers: taps_to_categorize_min = 3; taps_to_categorize_realistic = 6; ms_per_categorize_review_sheet_typical = ~2000-3000ms per Save & next tap (progress counter visibly advances); ms_per_categorize_inline_reliability = 4 of 6 in-place actions (McDonald's chip, Amazon full-select, Touchstone full-select, Sent +New) never visibly updated client-side within 5-10s+ of the tap despite the server returning 200 OK; only confirmed correct after a full page reload; rule_creation_saving_hang = >58s stuck on a disabled 'Saving…' button with zero feedback; the rule was actually created and auto-applied to 9 past transactions server-side despite the dialog never confirming it; rows_remaining_after = 10; rows_remaining_prior_month_hidden = 15; prior_month_uncategorized_pct = 83; dashboard_uncategorized_visibility = none - 'uncategorized'/'review'/'needs category' text absent from the dashboard both before and after clearing 9 transactions; pill_scope = current calendar month only, no household-wide rollup; small_tap_targets_transactions_page = 0; text_under_16px_pct_transactions = 55; categorized_this_session_inline = 6; categorized_this_session_review_sheet = 3; rules_created_this_session = 1; categories_created_this_session = 1.

I cold-open the dashboard expecting it to tell me there's work to do.
It doesn't.
I scan Net worth, Income/Spent/Saved, the account-card rail, 'Where it went' - nothing anywhere says a transaction needs a category.
I check this programmatically too: zero mentions of 'uncategorized', 'review', or 'needs category' on the page.
So I tap Transactions, and only there does a butter pill appear: '19 need a category or title - Review'.
That's the real starting line, and it's one full route change away from where I land every morning.
Categorizing in place mostly works the way the product model wants: tap Categorize on a row, a Quick category strip of chips drops in below it, tap a chip, done - no navigation, no lost scroll position.
I get three done this way (United Airlines, Spotify, McDonald's).
But twice, and then again with the full-select 'More…' path (Amazon, Touchstone Climbing) and the '+ New' path (Sent -> new 'Audit Transfers' category), the panel closes and the row snaps back to 'Uncategorized' with the Categorize button reappearing - no error, no toast, nothing red.
I nearly moved on thinking those taps failed.
Checking the network tab tells a different story: the server returned 200 every single time.
The category really did save - the client just silently failed to show it, sometimes only catching up after a full page reload.
That's a serious trust problem for a screen whose entire job is 'tap and see it happen.' The Review sheet (one row at a time, a progress counter, Save & next) works more predictably - a few seconds per row - but it's exactly the audit/triage step the brief says nobody should need, and I only reach it by tapping a pill most users would have no reason to find on day one.
The 'Always do this' rule feature genuinely exists and is well thought out - merchant, amount, account, share, and category all in one sheet, smartly pre-filled from the transaction I opened it from - but it's hidden behind a row button literally labeled 'Always share', which gives zero hint it also builds a category rule.
When I create a Uber rule, the Save button gets stuck on a disabled 'Saving…' for over a minute with no feedback; only by checking /rules directly do I learn it actually saved and even applied itself to 9 past Uber charges, quietly re-sharing 6 of them via the default 'Household ratio' setting.
Last surprise: the 'need a category' pill is entirely month-scoped.
Flipping back one month to July, 15 of 18 transactions (83%) are sitting uncategorized with zero indication anywhere - dashboard included - that backlog exists.
I finish having cleared 9 in place plus 1 via Review (10 total) and created one category and one merchant rule, leaving 10 uncategorized this month and the untouched July pile for later journeys.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Whole dashboard body (net worth card, Income/Spent/Saved tiles, account rail, 'Where it went') | Cold-opening the dashboard with 19 transactions sitting uncategorized gives zero signal that anything needs attention: no count, no banner, no CTA. Confirmed programmatically - the page's own text contains no occurrence of 'uncategorized', 'review', or 'needs category'. | The brief's whole product model is that categorizing is 'the user's whole daily job' - but the one screen the user opens every day never says the job exists. A user has to already know to tap into Transactions to discover the pill. | Add a dashboard tile/banner (near the top, above 'Where it went') reading e.g. '19 transactions need a category' that deep-links straight to the first uncategorized row on Transactions, and keep it in sync with the same count the Transactions pill shows. (src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx:257)

Network inspection showed every one of those POSTs actually returned 200, and the true category only appeared in the row after a full page reload. | The core promise of this screen is 'tap a chip, see it categorized, move on.' When the client silently fails to reflect a successful save, a user has no way to tell success from failure without opening devtools - they'll very plausibly re-tap, second-guess the app, or give up on a row they actually already finished. | Show the applied category optimistically the instant a chip/Apply/Create & apply is tapped (useOptimistic or local state update keyed off the response), and add a router.refresh()/cache-tag invalidation on the client after the server action resolves so the row never has to wait for an unrelated reload to catch up. (src/app/(app)/transactions/quick-categorize.tsx:38, src/app/(app)/transactions/actions.ts:251)
- [P1] Create rule button | Tapped Create rule on a fully-filled-out Uber rule (merchant, category Transportation, Household-ratio share, apply-to-past checked). The button flipped to a disabled 'Saving…' and stayed there for 58+ seconds with no toast, no error, and no new network request ever visible. Only by navigating to /rules directly did I learn the rule had in fact been created and had already applied itself to 9 past transactions, retroactively marking 6 of them as household-shared. | This is the exact 'set it once, stop re-categorizing this merchant forever' automation the product model exists to encourage, and its own save button hangs indefinitely with zero feedback - a real user would assume the app froze, likely force-closing the tab or re-submitting (risking duplicate rules) long before the change actually lands. | Fix the client so the rule-creation mutation's resolution actually closes the dialog and shows a confirmation ('Rule created - 9 matches found, 6 now shared'); add a client-side timeout/error state so a genuinely stuck request tells the user something went wrong instead of hanging forever on a disabled button. - [P1] 'need a category or title' pill / month navigator | The pill only counts the transactions in whichever month is currently in view. Paging back to July 2026 shows 18 transactions with 15 (83%) still Uncategorized - a backlog that's completely invisible from the August view, the pill, or the dashboard. | If a user only ever glances at 'this month's' pill, a large and growing pile of older uncategorized transactions can sit forever with no signal it exists - directly undermining 'the user's whole daily job is to categorize them.' | Make the pill (and any future dashboard banner) reflect a household-wide uncategorized count, not just the visible month, with a secondary line like '+15 more from earlier months' that jumps straight to the oldest unresolved month. - [P2] 'Always share' button | This button is the only entry point to merchant-rule creation (category + amount + account + sharing all in one sheet), but its label says nothing about categorization - the dialog it opens is titled 'Always do this', a completely different phrase from the button that triggers it. | A user specifically looking to stop re-categorizing a recurring merchant (the brief's 'Always do this' scenario) has no reason to associate a button called 'Always share' with that need; they'd have to click every row action once to discover it. | Rename the row button to match the dialog it opens (e.g. 'Always do this…') or split it into two clearly-labeled actions, and consider surfacing it as a suggestion the first time a merchant name repeats 2+ times uncategorized in the list. (src/app/(app)/transactions/row.tsx:299)
- [P2] Amount filter (defaults to 'Within a range') | Opening the rule sheet from a single $18.90 Uber charge pre-selects 'Within a range, $17.01 to $20.79' - a band derived from just that one transaction. Other Uber charges visible in the very same list ($5.40, $6.33, $23.40, $32.15) fall well outside it. | A user who accepts the smart-looking defaults without noticing the Amount radio gets a rule that looks complete but will barely fire for the recurring merchant it's named after - defeating the point of the automation. | Default new merchant rules to 'Any amount' (matching on merchant name alone), and only pre-fill a range if the user explicitly opts into amount-based matching. (screenshot: 12-rule-sheet-uber.png)
- [P2] Share = 'Household ratio' + 'Apply to matching past transactions' (both checked by default) | Creating the Uber rule with the default Share setting and the default 'apply to past' checkbox retroactively marked 6 already-existing, previously-personal Uber transactions as household-shared, with no distinct callout that sharing status - not just category - would be rewritten on existing rows. | Reshaping who a past expense is shared with is a materially different, more sensitive change than assigning it a category; bundling both under one 'apply to past transactions' checkbox risks a household member being surprised by transactions that suddenly count against a shared total. | Split the past-transaction backfill into two independent toggles - 'apply category to past matches' and 'apply sharing to past matches' - so a user can choose to categorize retroactively without silently reopening sharing on old rows.

After using 'Health' once for one row, it immediately appeared as a top chip on the very next uncategorized row, ahead of a less-recently-used default category. | This is exactly the 'chips are the categories I actually use' behavior the product model calls for, and it's already working well here. | No change needed here - worth reusing this same recency-adaptive pattern anywhere else the app offers a fixed pick-list (e.g. the account picker on manual transactions, or the parent-category select when creating a new category).

### J4 - Day 2, owner - "That Canadian Tire one - split out the GST."

There was no Canadian Tire row anywhere in August, so I picked the next best thing the brief allowed: an imported, round-amount charge - United Airlines, -$500.00, Transportation, on the Plaid Credit Card, already sitting in the list from an earlier journey.
I tapped Splits right on the row.
It didn't open a sheet or dialog, it just expanded in place below the row - fine, low-friction, exactly the 'stay in the list' model I'd want.
I tapped + Add split, picked Audit GST from the category dropdown (it exists only because an earlier session created it under Taxes - there's no built-in tax category), and typed 25.00 - 5% of 500, done in my head.
Nothing in the editor offered to do that math for me: no % button, no GST preset, no rate memory.
That's the expected friction the brief warned about, and it's real.
What wasn't expected: the 'Balanced' badge stayed green even though I'd now have two rows summing to $525 against a $500 total - it hadn't recalculated.
When I went and fixed the original Transportation row down to $475.00, the badge flipped to 'Remaining -$25.00' and offered 'Apply -$25.00 to last split' - which was wrong, my numbers by then already summed correctly.
I tapped it anyway to see what would happen; luckily it landed on the right answer.
I dug into the code afterward: the amount input only commits to the balance calculation on blur, not while typing, so the whole balance indicator is one keystroke behind reality during exactly the arithmetic task this journey is testing.
I fixed it back to 475/25, confirmed Balanced, and saved.
'Splits saved' appeared - as a plain paragraph, not the app's usual toast, so my toast watcher caught nothing.
Then the real scare: the still-open editor's own category dropdowns both snapped to '- Uncategorized -', amounts unchanged.
The row summary right above it, though, already read 'Split: Transportation + Audit GST' correctly, and budgets confirmed it - Transportation dropped by exactly $25, Taxes/Audit GST now show $25 spent.
Closing and reopening the editor showed the correct categories.
So it's a display bug, not data loss - but it's a display bug on the one screen a cautious user checks right after saving, and if they'd hit Save again in that state they'd have overwritten a correct split with two Uncategorized rows.
Changing the category from the row's own Edit form (not Splits) showed a good, proactive warning before I could commit - 'This transaction is split.
Changing the category here replaces every split with one row' - I cancelled.
Keyboard fit was the same story as every other journey: at a real 390x508 keyboard-open viewport, Save splits sits below the fold entirely.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] 'Balanced'/'Remaining' badge and the 'Apply $X to last split' shortcut inside the split editor | Right after typing the GST split's amount (25.00) with the original Transportation split still at 500.00, the badge kept showing '✓ Balanced' even though the two rows summed to $525 against a $500 total. After I corrected the Transportation row to 475.00, the badge flipped the other way to 'Remaining -$25.00' and offered 'Apply -$25.00 to last split' - both wrong, since 475+25 already equalled the $500 total. The badge is a beat behind whatever you just typed. | MoneyInput's onChange only updates local text state (setRaw); the onCents callback that feeds the parent's `rows` state - and therefore `currentSum`/`remaining`/`balanced` - fires only on blur. The split editor's live balance feedback is computed from state that lags one field-blur behind what's on screen, so exactly the arithmetic-checking task this feature exists for (see whether your split adds up) gives false answers mid-edit. | Fire onCents on every valid keystroke (debounced) in MoneyInput, not only onBlur, so `currentSum`/`remaining`/`balanced` in split-editor.tsx track the visible input text live. At minimum, recompute balance on input, not just blur. (src/components/ui/money-input.tsx:103, src/components/ui/money-input.tsx:104)
- [P1] Both split rows' category dropdowns in the still-open editor | After a successful Save splits, both category selects in the editor snap to '- Uncategorized -' while the amounts (475.00/25.00) stay correct. The row summary directly above (still on screen) correctly reads 'Split: Transportation + Audit GST', and the categorization is genuinely saved (confirmed on /budgets and by closing/reopening the same editor). But the form you're looking at right after the save you just made tells you your categorization is gone. | The split editor's local `rows` state isn't re-synced from the server's fresh, correctly-saved data after the Server Action + revalidatePath completes, so the open form momentarily shows a state that doesn't match reality. This is the same class of bug flagged in J2 (category add stuck for 6+ minutes on stale client state despite a 200 server response) and J3 (4 of 6 inline categorize actions visually reverted to Uncategorized despite succeeding server-side) - a recurring pattern where a successful mutation's own screen doesn't reflect the truth without a manual reload or re-open. | After a successful saveSplits, re-initialize the editor's `rows` state from the action's returned/re-fetched split data (or close the editor automatically on success, since the row summary above already shows the result) instead of leaving stale local state rendered over a form whose categories now silently don't match what got saved. I had to do 500 x 0.05 = 25 and 500 - 25 = 475 entirely in my head and type both numbers by hand. | This app's own stated model is that the user's whole job is categorizing in place and 'sometimes split out the GST' - the single named example of splitting in the product brief. Making that specific, common task require two independent mental-math calculations with zero assistance, while the one piece of live feedback available is unreliable (F1), is the app working against its own stated low-friction goal for its most explicitly named split scenario. | Add a 'Split off 5% GST' (or general 'split off X% as [category]') quick action next to Add split: given a target percentage and tax category, it should add the new row pre-filled with the computed amount and automatically reduce the original row by the same amount, leaving the split already balanced with zero typing beyond the percentage. (src/app/(app)/transactions/split-editor.tsx:193)
- [P3] Warning shown under the Category field when editing a split transaction's category directly from the row | Opening Edit (not Splits) on the already-split United Airlines row immediately shows, before any change is made: 'This transaction is split. Changing the category here replaces every split with one row.' Plain, specific, and shown proactively rather than only after a destructive tap. | This is a pattern worth copying elsewhere in the app - it warns before commitment, not via a modal confirm after the fact, and states the exact consequence in plain language. Contrast with J1/J3's silent Plaid-exit and rule-creation-hang, which give no equivalent warning. | No change needed here. I'm on the bus with maybe five minutes before my stop, trying to get through categorizing and logging a coffee, and true to the brief I get interrupted constantly - a call, a glance at another tab, the screen locking.
First I open the Review sheet and knock out two of five: Madison Bicycle Shop to Food, then a Starbucks to Audit Coffee.
Mid-third card I try to tap the Budgets tab to check something - and nothing happens.
Not a bounce back, not a close, literally nothing: the tap lands on the sheet's own scrim and is swallowed, because this bottom sheet (like every sheet in the app) is pinned flush to the screen bottom and outranks the tab bar.
I have to use the Close X instead.
Once out, my two saves held through a real tab-and-back and a hard reload - good, that part of the model works - but the sheet itself has no memory of being interrupted: reopening always says '1 of N, 0 categorized' again, even though the pill correctly dropped from 10 to 8.
Next I open Splits on the $100 ATM withdrawal, split it 70/30 into Transportation and Personal, and tap 'Hide splits' as my stand-in for a scrim dismiss.
Reopening: gone, back to one row at the full $100, Uncategorized.
Escape did nothing at all (it's not a real dialog), and a reload after refilling confirmed the same thing - any unsaved split is thrown away the instant the row collapses, no warning, no confirmation.
Then the manual entry test, which is where it got interesting.
I filled in $4.75, Audit Coffee, 'Flat white', and reached for the Budgets tab to test the switch-away case - and this time the tap actually submitted the transaction.
Turns out the 'Add transaction' sheet's own primary button sits at almost exactly the same screen coordinates as the Budgets tab icon underneath it, so my careful test of 'does switching tabs lose my draft' accidentally posted a real $4.75 charge against Audit Car Loan, the wrong account, because I'd never touched the account field.
Scrim, Close, and Escape all cleanly discarded later refills with no warning - consistent, if unforgiving.
Reload did the same.
Then I filled it in again, picked Audit Cash this time, and went offline before tapping save: a toast said 'You're offline.
We'll retry when you're back,' the dialog stayed open - but the Account field visibly snapped back to Audit Car Loan, Category to Uncategorized, and Description went blank, while only the dollar amount stayed put.
I came back online, waited ten seconds, and it turns out the retry actually used my original correct choices - Audit Cash, Audit Coffee, 'Flat white' all landed right.
So no data was really lost, but for those ten seconds the app was flatly lying to me about what it had.
Logging the $62.35 flat white afterward showed zero memory of the account or category I'd just used twice in a row - back to Audit Car Loan and Uncategorized again.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] The shared bottom-sheet panel (`fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-50`) vs. the fixed mobile tab bar (`fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-30`) | Every bottom sheet in the app is pinned flush to the very bottom of the screen, physically overlapping the fixed tab bar underneath it (dialog z-50 always wins over nav z-30). Tapping what LOOKS like the Budgets/Home tab while any sheet is open either (a) does nothing at all - no navigation, no dialog close, dead tap, confirmed on the Review sheet - or (b) if a real control from the sheet happens to sit at that exact pixel, that control fires instead. On the Add Transaction sheet, its own primary 'Add transaction' submit button sits at (197,774)-(370,820) in a 390x844 viewport, which overlaps the Budgets tab's hit area (233,786)-(310,844) almost completely. A tap aimed at the Budgets tab silently SUBMITTED the in-progress transaction instead - creating a real $4.75 'Flat white' charge against the wrong default account (Audit Car Loan) with zero confirmation. | The user's mental model is 'I'm switching tabs to check something.' Depending on which sheet happens to be open and how tall its content is, that exact same gesture either silently fails or silently commits data the user never confirmed. Neither outcome tells the user anything happened; the dangerous one (accidental submit) is indistinguishable from success until the user notices a stray transaction later. | In `src/components/ui/sheet.tsx`, either (1) give the scrim (line 102) and panel a max-height/bottom offset that stops above the mobile tab bar's height (58px + safe-area-inset-bottom) instead of `bottom-0`, so the tab bar is never covered and stays tappable while a sheet is open, or (2) if the tab bar must stay covered by design, add `pointer-events: none` to the nav while any Sheet is open and make the scrim itself the only thing at that z-level below the panel's real content (so a stray tap can only ever close the sheet, never fall through to a coincidentally-positioned submit button). Either fix removes the accidental-submit hazard; do not ship a fix that merely swallows the tap silently, since that still means the tab bar looks tappable but isn't. (budgeting-web-app/src/components/ui/sheet.tsx:102, budgeting-web-app/src/components/ui/sheet.tsx:110)

A toast correctly read 'You're offline. We'll retry when you're back.' and the dialog stayed open - but the on-screen Account field snapped back to the default 'Audit Car Loan', Category snapped back to '- Uncategorized -', and Description went blank. Only the Amount field ($4.75) visibly kept its value. This contradicts the form's own documented intent ('a network failure keeps the form filled') in `add-form.tsx`. After going back online and waiting 10s, the transaction DID land correctly with the original Audit Cash / Audit Coffee / 'Flat white' values (confirmed via the account card and the transactions list) - so no data was actually lost - but for as long as the user is offline, the screen actively lies about what it's about to save. | A user on a bus with patchy signal is exactly the scenario this feature exists for. Seeing your carefully-chosen account and category vanish the instant you tap Save reads as data loss, and a reasonable response is to re-enter everything (risking a duplicate once the honest queued retry also lands) or to distrust the app's 'we'll retry' promise entirely, even though the underlying retry mechanism is actually working correctly. | In `src/app/(app)/transactions/add-form.tsx`, stop whatever causes the uncontrolled `account_id`/`category_id`/`description` fields to visually revert on the offline branch (the `formRef.current?.reset()` call at line 60 is gated to only the confirmed-success path per the code, so the visual reset is coming from elsewhere - most likely a parent re-render/remount triggered by the offline-status banner; trace it and stop it) so that ALL fields, not just the React-controlled Amount, visibly retain what the user typed while a save is queued for retry. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/add-form.tsx:34, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/add-form.tsx:60)

A reload after refilling the same two rows confirmed the same total loss (row reverts to plain 'Uncategorized -$100.00', no split UI open at all). | This editor is not a dialog - it has no scrim, no Close button, and Escape does nothing (confirmed: pressing Escape left both rows exactly as typed, because there's no listener to catch it). The only way to leave it is to collapse the row, and collapsing is exactly what happens on any real-world interruption (switching apps, a notification, a phone call) since `showSplits` is a plain boolean that unmounts the child component. The product's whole premise is quick in-place edits that survive being interrupted; this one specific editor is the one place that silently punishes it. | Lift the split-row draft state (categories + amounts, not just the saved value) up to the parent `TransactionRow` (or a row-keyed cache) so it survives `showSplits` toggling to false and back to true, and clear it only on an explicit Save or an explicit 'discard' action - not on mere collapse. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/row.tsx:60, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/row.tsx:331)

The two prior categorizations ARE safely saved (confirmed surviving a tab-and-back and a hard reload), so this is not data loss - but the sheet gives no 'you already did 2 of these' cue, so a user resuming after a real interruption has no idea how far they got beyond re-reading the pill count. | The product model treats categorizing as a constant background task done in short bursts between interruptions - exactly this journey's premise. A queue that forgets its own progress every time it's reopened undercuts that by making every resume feel like starting over, even though the underlying data is fine. | Persist `pos`/`savedCount` (or derive them from which transaction IDs are still uncategorized vs. already handled this session) across a Sheet close/reopen instead of resetting `TriageQueue`'s local state on every mount, so reopening after an interruption picks up where the user left off. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/uncategorized-review.tsx:138, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/uncategorized-review.tsx:142)

Making them reselect the same account and category every single time, with zero memory even seconds later, is friction the app could trivially remove, and it compounds J5-F1's risk (a wrong-account accident is more likely when the field never remembers what you picked last). | Default the Account select to the last account used in this browser/session (or a per-user 'default manual account' setting) and default Category to the last-used category for this same description/merchant text, the same way the Review sheet's Quick-category chips already adapt to recency (see J3's `remembered_defaults`). ### J6 - Day 3, owner - "How much can I still spend on food this month? And how are we doing overall?"

Goal reached: yes. Taps 1, keystrokes 0, scrolls 5, decisions 0, route changes 2, return taps 1.
