audit_coffee_row_y_doc = 2674; scrolls_dashboard_to_where_it_went = 1; scrolls_budgets_to_audit_coffee = 4; taps_dashboard_to_budgets = 1; route_changes_dashboard_to_budgets = 1; month_period_clarity = Both /transactions and /budgets show an unambiguous 'August 2026' with </> arrows plus a static 'This month' badge - a clear pattern worth keeping/reusing elsewhere (e.g. next to dashboard's 'Where it went', which currently relies on the page-level header far above it for period context).

I cold-opened /dashboard wanting two numbers: how much food money I have left, and how the month is going overall.
The overall question got a partial zero-tap answer: Net worth $24,763.76 sits right at the top, and three tiles below it read Income $3K, Spent $12.1K, Saved -$9.1K, all without scrolling.
That negative Saved figure is a real, if uncomfortable, signal - I spent more than I earned this month - so 'overall' is at least attempted above the fold.
The food question got nothing.
No widget on the visible screen mentions any category, budget, or remaining amount.
I scrolled once and found 'Where it went', a bar of top categories - Debt payment, Food $1,022.18, Transportation, Health, Audit Transfers, Audit Food $240.40 - but every number there is a raw spend total with no budget next to it, so it still can't tell me what I have left.
I checked the code: the dashboard does have a dedicated 'Budget progress' widget (spent vs. budgeted this month) built and registered, but it isn't in the default layout - a user would have to find 'Edit dashboard' and turn it on themselves, and even then it's a single household-wide number, never per-category, so it could never have answered 'food' specifically anyway.
I also checked /transactions and found 8 transactions this month still sitting Uncategorized, including one called just 'Fun -$89.40' - plausibly a restaurant charge - and confirmed in the dashboard's own query that any split without a category_id is skipped when it builds those Food/Audit Food totals.
So the numbers I was reading could already be understated.
Tapping through to /budgets, the hero at the top reads 'This month $5,033.38 of $400.00, 1 of 13 categories budgeted, $4,633.38 over' in bold red - alarming, but almost entirely an artifact of 12 of 13 categories never having had a budget typed in; the code colors any category red the instant actual > budget, with budget defaulting to $0, so an untouched category looks exactly as 'over' as a real one.
I scrolled past ten identical-looking red 'Over by' rows (Housing, Transportation, Food, Health, Personal, Subscriptions, Taxes, Debt payment, Audit Transfers, Audit GST) before finally reaching Audit Food (Left $159.60 - the real food answer) immediately followed by Audit Coffee (Spent $88.87, Over by $38.87, clearly labeled, correctly summed 88.87-50=38.87).
The one number I actually needed - Audit Coffee's overage - was exact and legible once I got there; getting there was the whole problem.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Whole dashboard - no per-category budget widget in the default layout | Cold-opening the dashboard gives zero taps/zero scrolls to answer 'how much can I still spend on food'. Nothing above the fold mentions any category or budget at all. Scrolling once surfaces 'Where it went' (spend-only totals: Food $1,022.18, Audit Food $240.40), which still cannot answer the question because it carries no budgeted/remaining figure. A dedicated 'Budget progress' widget (spent-vs-budgeted this month) exists in the app's own widget registry but ships OFF by default - a user must discover 'Edit dashboard' and opt in - and even then it is a single household-wide total, never per-category, so it could not answer a food-specific question regardless. The only way to learn Audit Food has $159.60 left is a full route change to /budgets plus ~4 scrolls past 10 unrelated category rows. | The product's stated model is that the user's daily job is categorizing transactions in place, with balances and answers surfacing on their own - not hunting through a second page. 'How much can I still spend on X' is one of the two or three questions a budgeting app exists to answer instantly; today it requires a full navigation plus a long scroll, and even the escape hatch (Edit dashboard) can't get there because that widget is a total, not per-category. | Add a per-category remaining-budget module to the dashboard's DEFAULT_LAYOUT (not opt-in), showing the categories closest to or over their limit (e.g. the same rows /budgets already computes), above the fold. At minimum, turn 'budget-progress' on by default and add a per-category breakout so a single glance answers 'how much do I have left in X'. - [P1] Category rows with $0.00 budgeted (Housing, Transportation, Food, Health, Personal, Subscriptions, Taxes, Debt payment, Audit Transfers, Audit GST - 12 of 13 categories) | Every category that has never had a budget typed in (variance = actual - 0 = actual, always > 0 whenever there's any spend) renders 'Over by $X' in maple red, pixel-identical to a category that genuinely blew through a real budget (Audit Coffee: 'Over by $38.87', same red, same copy). ### J7 - Day 4 morning, owner - "Next morning: pull for new stuff, see what my rule did."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 27, keystrokes 0, scrolls 5, decisions 3, route changes 9, return taps 4.

I open Transactions expecting to catch up: pull down, see what's new, glance at whether my Uber rule from a couple days ago is still doing its job, clear the pile, and switch on notifications so I stop having to remember to check.
The pull gesture itself feels right - 'Pull to sync' becomes 'Release to sync' becomes 'Syncing...' and settles back - and I can confirm from the Bank sync page's log that it really did fire a sync (a fresh timestamped row appeared), which is reassuring since nothing on the Transactions page itself tells me that.
But finding 'Sync now' for my actual bank is its own small quest: the only sync-shaped thing on the Transactions page is behind the (...) 'More actions' menu, labelled 'Set up sync' - and it drops me into a whole Gmail Apps Script tutorial for a feature I never set up, nothing to do with the bank I linked.
I have to back out, guess Settings, scroll past Household/Members to 'Bank connections', and follow 'Manage bank sync' to finally find the real Sync now button, which gives quiet plain-text feedback ('Synced - 0 new, 0 enriched') and no toast.
Checking on my rule is worse: Rules shows two identical 'Uber' cards, both paused, with zero visual difference from an active rule - I only knew because I inspected the aria-pressed attribute.
One is my original rule (6 auto-shared, matches the story); a second, duplicate copy exists with different sharing settings.
Nothing on the transaction rows themselves ever says 'this was categorized by a rule' - I can only infer it from consistent categories.
Clearing the backlog goes fine mechanically but I hit the same silent-stale-UI bug three times running (row stays 'Uncategorized' after Apply, only fixed by a reload), and I discover there's no Income category anywhere in the list - my $2,450 payroll deposit has to go into 'Miscellaneous' next to junk-drawer spend.
The quick-category panel's own More/Apply controls also render right underneath the fixed tab bar, same blind spot flagged before in dialogs, now showing up in an inline panel too.
Notifications turned out to be a dead end in this browser: the actual 'Enable notifications' button that would trigger a permission prompt never even renders outside an installed iOS home-screen PWA - I only found that by reading the code - so the brief's expectation that 'the browser will likely deny' doesn't even apply here; there's no prompt to deny.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] 'Set up sync' link inside the (⋯) More actions sheet | The only sync-related control reachable from the Transactions page routes to /transactions/import/auto-setup - a Gmail email-alert forwarding tutorial for a feature this household never configured - instead of the real Plaid bank sync. There is no link anywhere on /transactions to the actual linked bank's Sync now button; I only found it by guessing Settings -> Bank connections -> Manage bank sync. | The product model says transactions arrive on their own via the linked bank and the user should never have to hunt for the pile. Instead the one 'sync' affordance on the page a user would tap sends them into an unrelated multi-step Gmail Apps Script setup guide. | In transactions/page.tsx, gate the SyncNowButton fallback on whether the household has a Plaid item connected (not only on hasSyncUrl for the Gmail flow): when a bank is linked, render a direct 'Sync now' control that calls the Plaid sync action (same one used on /transactions/import/plaid-setup), and only fall back to the 'Set up sync' Gmail link when no bank is linked at all. - [P2] 'Enable notifications' button / standalone-mode gate | The Notifications section shows only a static message - 'Add Maple to your home screen first (Share -> Add to Home Screen), then open it from the icon to turn notifications on' - with no button, in any non-standalone browser context. Verified in code: the actual Enable-notifications button (which calls the browser's native Notification.requestPermission()) only renders when window.matchMedia('(display-mode: standalone)').matches or navigator.standalone is true - there is no branch for browsers (desktop Chrome/Edge/Firefox, Android Chrome) that support standard Web Push without any home-screen installation at all. Notification.permission stayed 'default' throughout - no browser permission dialog was ever offered to accept or deny. | The brief expected 'browser will likely deny' the permission prompt, but on this platform there is no prompt to deny at all - the feature is invisible outside an installed iOS Safari PWA, even though Chrome/Edge/Firefox all support the exact same Push API this code already calls. A user who reads 'notify me the moment a transaction lands' and checks every box below it has no way to know none of it can ever fire without a completely separate, unmentioned installation ritual. | In notifications.tsx, split the standalone check: keep the iOS-specific 'Add to Home Screen' messaging only for iOS Safari (detect via userAgent/navigator.standalone), and show the real 'Enable notifications' button directly in any other Push-API-supporting browser (window.matchMedia('(display-mode: standalone)').matches should not gate Chrome/Edge/Firefox, which support Web Push in a normal tab). (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/setup/notifications.tsx:28, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/setup/notifications.tsx:117) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J7\10-notifications-section.png)
- [P2] Per-rule action row (Edit / Apply to last 12 months / Pause / Delete) | There is no way to see or revert what a specific past rule application changed. If a rule mis-fires (wrong category, wrong sharing), the only recovery path is to manually find and re-edit every affected transaction one at a time - there's no 'undo this rule's last backfill' or per-application history. | The brief specifically asks whether a wrong rule hit can be undone; right now the answer is no, and the cost of a bad rule scales with how many transactions it already touched, with no batch remedy. | Log each rule application as a batch (rule_id, transaction_ids, prior category/sharing values, timestamp) and add an 'Undo last apply' action on the rule card that restores the prior values for exactly that batch. (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J7\05-rules-duplicate-uber-paused.png)
- [P3] Sync feedback (pull-to-refresh label + Sync now inline text) | Pull-to-refresh's mechanics are good (clear 3-stage label, and it does trigger a real backend sync - confirmed via a fresh timestamped row in the Bank sync page's Recent syncs log) but the Transactions page itself never shows a result once the gesture settles - it just reverts to 'Pull to sync' with no '0 new' or similar summary. The explicit Sync now button on the Bank sync page does show a result ('Synced - 0 new, 0 enriched.') but only as a plain paragraph next to the button, not the app's usual role="status" toast pattern (a toast watcher caught zero events across both actions). | Worth flagging both ways: the pull gesture itself is a pattern other flows in this app should copy (unambiguous 3-state label, real backend effect) - but its silence on the page that triggered it, plus the still-unfixed toast gap already noted in J1b-F6/J4-F5, mean neither sync path gives the confirmation a user glancing at their phone actually needs. | Surface the sync result inline on /transactions itself after a pull (e.g. a brief '0 new' / '+3 new' toast using the existing role="status" pattern), and switch the Bank sync page's 'Synced - X new, Y enriched' text to that same toast component instead of a plain paragraph. (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J7\04-bank-sync-page.png)

Could not test: Whether the real iOS Safari 'Add to Home Screen' -> Enable notifications flow actually shows and correctly handles the native permission prompt - untestable from this desktop-emulated Playwright session; would need a real iPhone/Safari. | Root cause of the duplicate paused Uber rules (whether J3's 58s+ 'Saving…' hang double-submitted the rule creation, or something else paused both copies afterward) - would need server-side rule creation/update timestamps or logs to confirm. | Whether 'Sync all' on the Bank sync page (as opposed to the per-bank 'Sync now' I used) behaves any differently - not exercised this session. | Whether Push notifications, if actually enabled on a real device, correctly fire for each of the five preference categories (transaction, budget overage, failed bank alert, settlement) - none could be tested since no subscription could be created.

### J8 - Day 5, owner, on the budgets screen - "Log a cash purchase from wherever I am."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 22, keystrokes 51, scrolls 0, decisions 5, route changes 3, return taps 1.
Key numbers: taps_from_budgets_to_saved = 5; taps_from_accounts_to_saved = 5; route_change_wait_ms_budgets_to_transactions = 3000; route_change_wait_ms_accounts_to_transactions = 3000; return_taps = 1; save_hang_ms_entry_1_groceries_from_budgets = 48000; save_hang_ms_entry_2_groceries_from_accounts = 2000; save_hang_ms_entry_3_payroll = 18000; save_hang_note = 2 of 3 submits hung 18-48s+ on a disabled 'Saving…' button with zero feedback while the mutation had already succeeded server-side; 1 of 3 resolved in ~2s - same code path, inconsistent latency traced to revalidate() fanning out to 6 revalidatePath calls per mutation (actions.ts:26-32); inline_edit_latency_ms = 2000; inline_edit_toast_shown = false; delete_row_removed_from_ui_within_8s = false; delete_confirmed_gone_after_reload = true; toast_count_this_session = 0; account_select_default = Audit Car Loan (first option alphabetically); duplicate_groceries_rows_created = 2; add_txn_submit_covered_by_336px_keyboard = true; add_txn_submit_keyboardFit = {"viewportH":844,"keyboardTop":508,"submit_y":774,"submit_bottom":820,"submitVisible":false,"submitCoveredByKeyboard":true}; budgets_audit_food_before = 240.4; budgets_audit_food_after_this_journey = 1490.4; budgets_audit_food_math_check = 240.40 + 1250.00 = 1490.40 (correct, confirmed on a fresh /budgets page load); delete_confirm_dialog_text = Delete this transaction? / The transaction and its splits will be removed. This can't be undone.; delete_confirm_button_layout = Delete (destructive, y=727-773) above Cancel (secondary, y=781-827), both full-width 349x46.

I started on /budgets, thumb hovering over the centre + in the tab bar.
Tapped it expecting a quick add-transaction sheet right here - instead the whole app navigated to /transactions?add=1, and I watched the full 36-row transaction list render underneath the dialog before I could type anything.
That is a real page load I did not ask for just to log ten dollars of groceries.
I filled Amount 1250, Category Audit Food, Description 'Groceries big' - the Account field was already sitting on 'Audit Car Loan', not Cash, so a cash-shaped purchase silently posted against a loan account.
Tapped Add transaction.
The button flipped to a disabled 'Saving...' and just sat there.
No toast, no spinner, no error - I watched it for 48 seconds before giving up and reloading, and there was my transaction, correctly saved, the whole time.
I repeated the exact same steps from /accounts - same full-page hop to /transactions, same 'Groceries big' -$1,250.00 - and this time it saved in about two seconds.
Same button, same form, wildly different latency; I later found the code fans every save out to six revalidatePath calls before the promise resolves, which explains the variance.
Logging Payroll (Received, $2,500, Audit Cash) hung again, 18+ seconds on 'Saving...', and again the data was already there when I reloaded.
Editing one Groceries row inline (tap Edit, change 1250.00 to 125.00, tap Save) worked fast this time but gave me zero confirmation either way - no toast, no checkmark, nothing to tell me the edit took, and no undo if I had mistyped.
Deleting that same row was the one moment of real reassurance: 'Delete this transaction?
The transaction and its splits will be removed.
This can't be undone.' - clear, unambiguous, full-width buttons.
But the row itself refused to disappear from my screen even after 8+ seconds; only a reload proved it was actually gone.
One tap got me back to Budgets.
I did get every entry logged and cleaned up, but I never once trusted the screen in front of me to tell me the truth about what had just happened - I had to reload after almost every mutation to find out.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Tab-bar centre "+" (Add transaction) | Tapping the + from /budgets or /accounts triggers a full route change to /transactions?add=1, which renders the entire (36+ row) transaction list underneath the dialog before I can type anything. The identical button, tapped from /transactions itself, opens the same dialog in place with zero navigation and zero extra render. | Logging a quick cash purchase from wherever you are - the journey's whole stated goal - costs a full page load and a visible content swap the user never asked for, purely because of which screen they happened to be on when they reached for +. | Render AddTransactionForm as a shell-level overlay/portal mounted once in the app layout (as it effectively already behaves when triggered from /transactions), so tapping + from any route opens the dialog in place without a router.push to /transactions first. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:401) (screenshot: 02-add-txn-dialog-from-budgets.png)
- [P1] "Add transaction" submit button | After tapping Add transaction, the button flips to a disabled 'Saving…' and stays there with no toast, no spinner beyond the static label, and no error - observed 48+ seconds and 18+ seconds on two of three submits this session (the third resolved in ~2s). In every hung case the transaction was already correctly saved server-side seconds after the tap; only a hard reload revealed that. | A user has no way to distinguish a slow-but-working save from a failed one. Waiting 48 seconds on a disabled button with no feedback reads as broken, and the natural response - reload or resubmit - risks a duplicate charge (see J8-F7) precisely because the app gives no signal the first attempt already landed. | Stop gating the button's pending state on the full round trip through revalidate(). Either (a) make the six revalidatePath calls in actions.ts fire via a non-blocking after()/waitUntil() so the action promise resolves as soon as the insert is committed, or (b) show a toast/optimistic row the moment the insert succeeds, independent of how long downstream revalidation takes. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/actions.ts:26, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/actions.ts:76) (screenshot: 04-add-txn-saving-hang.png)
- [P1] Delete (row) + confirm dialog | After confirming Delete on the -$125.00 Groceries big row, the row stayed visible in the list for 8+ seconds of active waiting with no change, no spinner, nothing. The transaction had actually been deleted server-side; only reloading the page removed it from view. | A delete that visibly does nothing invites a second tap on Delete (harmless here since the row is already gone server-side, but the user has no way to know that) or a belief that the delete silently failed - eroding trust in every other destructive action in the app. | Same root fix as J8-F2: deleteTransaction's revalidate() call (actions.ts:313) should not block the client from optimistically removing the row from local state the moment the delete action resolves at the database layer; don't wait on all 6 revalidatePath calls to update the visible list. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/actions.ts:303, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/actions.ts:313) (screenshot: 12-after-delete.png)
- [P2] Row inline Edit -> amount field -> Save | Editing a transaction's amount inline (Edit expands the row into its own form, Save collapses it) gives zero confirmation on success - no toast, no checkmark, no 'Saved' text - and there is no undo control anywhere near the row. | The one moment a user most wants reassurance - after correcting a wrong dollar figure - is exactly when this app says nothing. A mistyped correction (e.g. 125.00 fat-fingered as 1250.00) would look identical to a successful save and there is no one-tap way to revert it. | Fire a brief toast ('Amount updated to $125.00') on successful inline save, matching the pattern already used elsewhere in the app for other confirmations, and add an inline 'Undo' action to that toast for at least a few seconds after the edit. (screenshot: 10-inline-edit-saved-no-feedback.png)
- [P2] Account select | The Account field in the Add Transaction form defaults to 'Audit Car Loan' (first option alphabetically) on every open, regardless of which account was used moments earlier in the same session. All three transactions logged this run needed the account manually changed or were left posting against the wrong account by default. | In a product whose stated model treats manual entry as the exception for cash, the one field a cash entry can't skip - which account the cash came from - fights a persistently wrong default every single time, adding a decision + a select-tap to what should be the fastest path in the app. Confirmed as the same unfixed defect noted in J1 and J5. | Default the Account select to the account used in the user's most recent manual transaction this session (or, absent that, to a dedicated 'Cash' account if one exists), not to the first option in alphabetical order. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/add-form.tsx:111) (screenshot: 03-add-txn-filled.png)
- [P2] Add transaction (no duplicate check) | Logging the identical transaction (amount, category, description) from /budgets and then again from /accounts within about a minute created two indistinguishable '-$1,250.00 Groceries big · Audit Food · Audit Car Loan' rows with no warning, prompt, or soft nudge of any kind. | Combined with J8-F2's silent 'Saving…' hang, a real user who reloads or retaps after a stalled save (reasonably believing the first attempt failed) will produce exactly this: a duplicate charge with zero safety net to catch it. | Add a lightweight same-day duplicate check on transaction create (same account, same amount, same or blank description within e.g. 5 minutes) that surfaces a non-blocking 'You just logged a similar $1,250.00 transaction - continue anyway?' confirmation rather than saving silently. (screenshot: 06-second-groceries-added.png)
- [P3] "Delete this transaction?" dialog | This confirm dialog is a genuinely good pattern: clear question, one plain-language sentence stating exactly what is lost ('The transaction and its splits will be removed. This can't be undone.'), two full-width 46px buttons with no reliance on color alone. | Worth calling out as the template the rest of the app's destructive confirms (rules, categories, accounts) should copy - several other flows in earlier journeys used vaguer or missing confirmation copy. | No change needed here; propagate this exact copy pattern (plain-language consequence sentence + explicit "can't be undone") to other destructive confirms in the app. (screenshot: 11-delete-confirm-dialog.png)

Could not test: Exact millisecond timing of the /budgets -> /transactions and /accounts -> /transactions route changes - only bounded by manual 3s waits, not precisely instrumented per-navigation. | Root cause of why 2 of 3 saves hung 18-48s+ while the 3rd resolved in ~2s with identical code path - traced to the shared revalidate() fan-out in actions.ts but would need server-side timing/APM to confirm which of the 6 revalidatePath calls (or Supabase round trip) is the variable cost. | Whether the /accounts screen's Add-transaction entry point differs from /budgets' in any way beyond the confirmed identical navigation to /transactions?add=1 - not tested from any other screen (e.g. /rules, /setup). | Whether reaching /accounts from the tab bar's 'More' sheet in a real single-thumb session (2 taps: More -> Accounts) changes any of the above timings - this session navigated to /accounts directly by URL for efficiency rather than through the in-app More sheet.

### J9a - Day 7, owner - "Invite my partner."

I cold-opened the dashboard, then went straight to Settings (/setup) since that's where 'household' stuff has always lived in this app.
The Members card was already on screen without much scrolling: an email field, an Access dropdown defaulted correctly to 'Member', and an Invite button.
I typed audit-partner@budgeting-app.local, left Access alone since Member was already selected, and tapped Invite.
Nothing happened - the tap landed on the fixed bottom tab bar's '+' button underneath instead, because the Invite button sat right where that bar covers the screen.
I had to scroll down about 150px to pull the button clear of the tab bar before a second tap actually registered.
That's a real 'my first tap did nothing and I don't know why' moment on a phone.
Once it went through, the app told me it couldn't send the email - and the message it showed me was alarming: a raw backend error naming Resend, telling me to 'verify a domain at resend.com/domains', and printing my own email address back at me in parentheses.
That's not something a household owner should ever see; it reads like a leaked server log, not a product message, and it exposes my personal email in a household members panel.
Below that was a copyable one-time link, valid 7 days, which I grabbed.
Copying it gave a nice 'Copied' label flip for a couple seconds - genuinely good feedback, the kind other flows in this app should copy.
Next I went to set a 60/40 split like the brief asked, but the Default split card still only listed me (Playwright, 100%) - my just-invited partner doesn't show up there at all, because they're not a real member yet, just a pending invite.
There's no note anywhere on the page saying 'they'll appear here once they accept', so I'd have had no idea why the task felt undoable if I hadn't checked the underlying data.
I also noticed the invited row's email gets truncated with an ellipsis in the members list, so I can't glance-check I typed it right without re-reading the earlier confirmation text.
Finally, I looked at 'close day' - it's a day-of-month selector (1-28) with a one-line explanation: everyone gets pushed what they owe on that day, and you can close early from Settlements.
I read that as: it's the day the shared-expense tab resets and gets tallied, not a bill due date.
I left it on the seeded default (Day 28) since nothing asked me to change it.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Invite button (Members card) | My first tap landed exactly where the green Invite button is drawn, but nothing happened - no submission, no error, no state change. Only after scrolling ~150px to pull the button clear of the bottom tab bar did a second tap actually register. | The <main> content area on mobile has no bottom padding reserved for the fixed, 59px-tall Primary tab bar (position:fixed, z-30). Whenever a card's primary action lands near the bottom of the current scroll position - as the Members card's Invite button did here - it renders directly under the tab bar's hit box, and the nav's own '+' button (which sits on top) intercepts the tap instead. | Add bottom padding/spacer to the mobile <main> equal to the tab bar height plus safe-area inset (e.g. pb-[76px]) so no interactive element can ever be geometrically covered by the fixed nav's hit area. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:521, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:544) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J9a\04-invite-button-blocked-by-tabbar.png)
- [P1] Members card - 'could not send the email' fallback message | After a successful Invite tap, the panel read: "We could not send the email (You can only send testing emails to your own email address (bespalkodeagan@gmail.com). To send emails to other recipients, please verify a domain at resend.com/domains, and change the `from` address to an email using this domain.). Share this link instead - it works once and expires in 7 days." This is a raw backend/vendor error shown verbatim to the household owner, naming the email provider (Resend), giving developer-facing remediation steps, and printing my own personal email address back at me. | The server action stores the send failure's raw message untouched and the client component interpolates it directly into user-facing copy with no sanitization or classification. | Catch and classify the email-send failure server-side; show a generic, branded fallback (e.g. "We couldn't email this invite - share the link instead.") and log the raw provider error only server-side. Never render any part of the provider's error text or the account owner's email in the invite UI. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/setup/invite-actions.ts:98, budgeting-web-app/src/components/invite-result.tsx:30) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J9a\05-invite-link-result.png)
- [P2] Default split card | Right after successfully inviting my partner, I scrolled down to set a 60/40 default split as the brief asked. The card still showed only 'Playwright - 100% of every shared expense' - a single row. My newly-invited partner does not appear anywhere in this section, and nothing on the page explains why or says they'll show up once they accept. | SplitWeightsForm is built from the `members` table (filtered on !archived_at), and a pending invite is not a row in `members` until the invitee actually signs up and accepts - so there is genuinely no member to assign a weight to yet. | Add a pending-invite row to the Default split card (e.g. greyed out, "audit-partner@... - joins once they accept") so the owner understands why the ratio can't be set yet, or let the owner pre-set the intended split ratio at invite time so it's ready the moment the invite is accepted. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/setup/page.tsx:122, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/setup/page.tsx:125)
- [P3] Invited member row, Members list | The invited email 'audit-partner@budgeting-app.local' renders truncated with an ellipsis ('audit-partner@budgeting-a...') in the Members list row, so I can't glance-verify I invited the right address without scrolling back up to the earlier confirmation text. | The row's email <span> has a fixed truncate class inside a narrow flex column (container ~180px vs text width ~212px at 14px font). | Either wrap the email onto a second line instead of truncating, or widen the column / drop the row's other elements to give the email more room, since correctness of the invited address matters more here than in most truncated-text contexts. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/setup/members-list.tsx:232)

Could not test: Whether the invite link actually lets a second person sign up and appear as a real Member (would require completing sign-up as a second browser/identity, not attempted per audit rules). | Whether the Default split card would then correctly show and let me set 60/40 once the partner accepts. | Whether this raw Resend/domain-verification error text is environment-specific to this sandbox or would also show in a misconfigured production deployment. | Whether there is any alternate path (not found) to pre-set a split ratio for a pending, not-yet-accepted invite.

### J9b - Day 7, partner on their own phone - "Join the household and mark what I paid for."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 21, keystrokes 90, scrolls 2, decisions 5, route changes 7, return taps 0.
Key numbers: invite_link_used = http://localhost:3000/invite/ayMq3JwPyAy8j_Q8Mt0UZdyDvSGiduB4uni5-tFG6EY; onboarding_steps = 3; taps_invite_to_dashboard = 13; taps_dashboard_to_categorized_and_shared = 8; plaid_accounts_available_prechecked = 14; plaid_continue_completed = false; member_own_accounts_after_session = 0; categorize_apply_visible_lag_observed = true; categorize_apply_immediate_ui_state = UNCATEGORIZED (stale); categorize_apply_confirmed_state_after_reopen = Miscellaneous; share_toggle_feedback = immediate optimistic 'Sharing...' label, then 'SHARED' badge; running_tally_after_share = All square (unchanged - Joint account, no debt created); income_category_available = false; categories_seen_total = 15; shared_source_accounts_all_joint = true; transactions_needing_category_at_session_start = 4; onboarding_bank_link_pill_text = 1 OF 10 BANKS LINKED; onboarding_bank_link_pill_shown_at_zero_linked = true.

I open the invite link cold, as a second phone.
The landing page is genuinely reassuring for a first-time visitor: "Playwright has invited you to track your money together.
Your own accounts stay private; the household sees only joint accounts and the transactions you share." That one sentence answers the exact anxiety a new household member has (what will they see of MY money?) before I even sign in.
I tap "I already have one" and land on a normal sign-in form; the URL carries a next= redirect back to the invite token, so I don't have to re-find the invite after auth - good.
Signing in drops me straight into onboarding/welcome with no separate accept/confirm screen - the invite was silently accepted as part of sign-in.
"You are in.
Here is the deal." restates the same three rules (your accounts stay yours, joint accounts are shared, you choose what to split) - a little repetitive right after the invite page said the same thing, but not painful.
Continue -> name step, prefilled with "audit-partner" pulled from my email; I clear it to "Audit Partner" and continue.
Step 3 is bring-in-your-money: a Plaid button and a manual add-by-hand form shown together on one screen.
I try Plaid first since the brief says sandbox now works - Tartan Bank, user_good/pass_good, 14 accounts come back all pre-checked - but the Plaid Link iframe's own "Continue" would not advance no matter how I tapped it (reload resets cleanly, so nothing was left half-linked - I could not tell if this is a real product defect or my tooling misfiring on the cross-origin iframe, so I'm not scoring it as a finding, just flagging it unverified).
I fall back to Skip for now.
Dashboard: "Bonjour, Audit." - six accounts already sitting there, all correctly labelled Shared, all the owner's Plaid-linked institution.
This is exactly the promise from the invite screen keeping itself: I did nothing and the joint household accounts are just there.
Net worth card, P/A member avatars, all present.
Transactions is where the day-to-day happens, and it's also where I hit a real bug: a fixed "Install Maple" card sits above the tab bar and does not scroll with the list - so depending on scroll position it lands directly on top of whatever row (or, worse, the quick-category chip picker) happens to be there, visually and functionally blocking taps underneath it.
I only fully understood this after the tool's own click-safety check reported an account card as "covered by <div.maple-chrome.fixed>", and a screenshot of the category picker showed the banner sitting squarely over the Audit GST / Miscellaneous / More / +New chips.
For a page whose entire job is tapping rows to categorize them, a randomly-positioned blocking overlay is a real tax on the core loop.
Once I dismiss it, categorizing works: I open the $5,850 Gusto payroll deposit's Categorize picker, and the quick chips are all expense categories (Transportation, Food, Health, GST, transfers, Miscellaneous) - there's no Income category anywhere in the full list either, so a real paycheque has no honest home and I file it as Miscellaneous.
Picking Apply gave zero visible feedback - the row still read UNCATEGORIZED in text a moment later - and only reopening it a few seconds after confirmed it had actually saved.
That is the same silent-save latency J8 already caught on the owner side; seeing it again independently as the partner says it's not a one-off.
Sharing is the nicer half of this visit: tapping Share on the Kfc row gave an immediate optimistic "Sharing..." label, then settled to a SHARED badge - fast, visible, no ambiguity, a good contrast to the categorize-apply silence.
On /shared the running tally reads "All square" even after my share, which makes sense once I looked at the source-account list: every account in this household is Joint, so sharing a transaction that's already paid from joint money doesn't create a debt between anyone - there's nothing to owe.
That's a reasonable design once you see it, but the UI doesn't say it anywhere; a first-time partner sharing an expense and seeing the tally not move at all could easily read that as broken instead of correct.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Fixed 'Install Maple' hint card (position: fixed, z-20) above the mobile tab bar | The install-hint banner does not scroll with the page. As I scrolled the transaction list, it sat squarely on top of whatever row was at that vertical band - once directly over the Aug 24 Uber row, and again directly over the quick-category chip picker's Audit GST / Miscellaneous / More / +New buttons when I opened Categorize on the Gusto payroll deposit. The tool's own click-safety check independently confirmed the same thing on the dashboard: an account card tap was rejected because the click point was 'covered by <div.maple-chrome.fixed>'. | The core daily job of this app is tapping a transaction row to categorize or split it in place. A persistent floating card that can land on top of the exact controls you need to tap - with no guarantee of where in the scroll it ends up - is direct friction on that one task, and it can silently eat taps (the user thinks they tapped 'Miscellaneous' and instead tapped the install card). | Stop reserving space only at the bottom of the page (--maple-hint-h padding helps only the very last row). Either: (a) give the hint's outer wrapper pointer-events:none and only the inner white card pointer-events:auto so taps that land on its transparent margin fall through, (b) auto-hide the hint whenever a row's action panel (Categorize / Splits / Edit) is expanded, or (c) replace the persistent fixed card with a one-time auto-dismissing toast that never sits mid-list for more than a few seconds. ### J9c - Day 7, owner - "Split the groceries with my partner and see who owes whom."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 12, keystrokes 0, scrolls 2, decisions 2, route changes 5, return taps 2.
Key numbers: outstanding_now_dollars = 42.11; running_tally_sentence = Audit Partner owes Playwright $42.11; period_open_since = Aug 1, 2026; period_close_date = Aug 28; todays_date = Aug 29, 2026; period_overdue_days = 1; source_account_dropdown_options = 17; source_account_default = Audit Visa - Mine (first credit_card-type account by query order); taps_more_sheet_to_shared_page = 2; shared_page_in_bottom_tab_bar = false; share_toggle_toast_text = Shared with the household.; share_toggle_default_split = even household ratio, applied instantly, no picker; row_splits_button_feature = category split (e.g. GST), distinct from cost-sharing split; cost_split_editor_location = /shared -> account filter -> transaction row -> Edit split; kfc_split_line_text = Playwright owes $250.00 · Audit Partner owes $250.00; loblaws_split_line_text = Playwright keeps $42.12 · Audit Partner owes $42.11; outstanding_chip_contrast_ratio = 4.07; outstanding_chip_contrast_needed = 4.5; clear_split_tap_target_px = 38x44; small_text_pct_under_16px = 78; transactions_stat_tile_label_overflow_px = 2.

I start on /transactions looking for a grocery row to share.
Real Plaid-sandbox merchants here are the usual generic set (Uber, Starbucks, McDonald's, United Airlines) - the only genuinely grocery-named rows (Loblaws, Safeway, Costco) sit on the manual 'Audit Visa' test account rather than a live Plaid account, so I use Loblaws -$84.23 as my grocery row.
Right on the row, without opening anything, there are five actions in a line: Splits, Edit, Share, Always share, Delete - no swipe, no overflow menu.
I tap Share.
Instantly the row gets a green SHARED badge, the button flips to Unshare, and a toast says 'Shared with the household.' No dialog, no confirmation, no split picker - it just committed a 50/50 split on the spot.
That's the right instinct for this product, but it also means there is zero hint from the row that a custom ratio is even possible.
Next I go hunting for /shared.
It is not in the bottom tab bar (Home / Transactions / + / Budgets / More), so I open More and scan a grid of thirteen tiles grouped by section - I find it under 'SPLIT & SETTLE' as 'Shared expenses', two taps total from anywhere in the app.
The page itself is genuinely good: a plain-language sentence up top, 'Audit Partner owes Playwright $42.11' - exactly the kind of thing I could read out loud.
I try switching the 'Source account' dropdown (17 accounts, no 'All' option) to see if the tally is scoped to one account - reassuringly it is not; the headline number stays $42.11 no matter which of the 17 accounts I pick.
But the transaction list underneath IS scoped per-account, and it defaults to a manual/personal account rather than anything obviously relevant, so actually reviewing which transactions are and aren't shared means clicking through up to 17 filters one at a time.
I poke at 'Edit split' on the already-shared Kfc row (a joint-account transaction) to see if custom ratios exist at all - they do, a proper dollar-amount split editor with a 'Split equally' shortcut - but reaching it took: More, Shared expenses, pick the right account, find the transaction again, Edit split.
Four screens deep for something the row's own Share button hides completely.
I also notice the Kfc row's inline summary reads 'Playwright owes $250.00 · Audit Partner owes $250.00' - both people 'owe', with no net direction - quite different from Loblaws' clear 'Playwright keeps $42.12 · Audit Partner owes $42.11'.
I cancel out without saving either edit.
Back on /transactions I also open the row's separate 'Splits' button (category splitting, not cost-sharing) to confirm it's a different feature entirely, then close it.
Goal reached - I can absolutely state who owes whom - but reaching the deeper controls and reading a single joint-account split both took more hunting than the headline number did.

Findings on the way:

- [P2] Source account combobox (17 options, no 'All accounts') | The headline 'Outstanding now' / 'Running tally' numbers are correctly household-wide (verified: unchanged at $42.11 across every account I selected), but the transaction list and 'Total shared / this account' stat beneath it are scoped to exactly one account at a time, and the dropdown offers no aggregate 'All accounts' choice. To actually review which of the household's transactions are and aren't flagged shared, the owner has to click through up to 17 separate account filters one by one - the default selection is just the first credit-card-type account returned by the query, not anything meaningfully relevant. | The product model says the user never wants a separate review/audit step; browsing 'what's shared so far' by paging through 17 individual account filters is exactly that kind of manual audit, on a page whose whole promise is 'split fairly, settle quickly.' | Add an 'All accounts' entry as the default option in the Source account combobox that unions transactions across every account (Joint and Mine) into one list with the same Share/Unshare controls, and default the switcher to it instead of the first credit-card account by query order. (src/app/(app)/shared/page.tsx:303, src/app/(app)/shared/account-switcher.tsx:10)
- [P2] Per-transaction split summary line on a joint-account shared row (e.g. Kfc) | On the Loblaws row (a 'Mine' account, Playwright is the named payer) the split line correctly reads 'Playwright keeps $42.12 · Audit Partner owes $42.11'. On the Kfc row (a Joint/'Shared account' transaction with no single payer) the equivalent line instead reads 'Playwright owes $250.00 · Audit Partner owes $250.00' - both members shown as owing, with no net direction stated for that specific transaction. | The brief's own test is whether the number is 'labelled clearly enough to say out loud' - the top-level tally passes that test, but reading any individual joint-account split out loud produces a confusing 'both of us owe $250' sentence instead of a clear direction, even though the transaction has already been paid for. | In the no-payer branch, either compute and show a net line (e.g. 'Split evenly - no balance owed on this one' when payer_id is null and it was paid from a joint account) or explicitly label the amounts as 'share' rather than 'owes' for both members so it reads as a cost breakdown, not a debt. (src/app/(app)/shared/row.tsx:218, src/app/(app)/shared/row.tsx:224)
- [P2] Share button on the transaction row | Tapping Share commits an even household-ratio split immediately (confirmed in code: 'One-tap share / unshare by the household ratio'). There is no affordance on the row itself - no long-press, no chevron, nothing - suggesting a custom ratio is possible. Reaching the actual split-ratio editor requires: More -> Shared expenses -> pick the right one of 17 accounts in a dropdown -> find the same transaction again in that filtered list -> Edit split. | The row already exposes a separate 'Splits' button for category-splitting (confirmed to be the GST-style split, not cost-sharing) right where the model wants it - one tap, no navigation. Cost-sharing ratio has no equivalent shortcut, so a user who wants anything other than 50/50 gets no signal from the row that a deeper path even exists. | Add a small affordance next to Share on the row - e.g. a chevron/kebab that opens 'Share evenly' (current one-tap behavior, unchanged) vs 'Split custom...' which jumps straight to that transaction's split editor without the account-switcher detour. (src/app/(app)/transactions/row.tsx:67)
- [P3] Running tally period card ('Closes Aug 28 · every month on the 28th') | Today is Aug 29, 2026 (one day past the stated close date), yet the period card still shows an open, un-closed state ('No closed periods yet', active 'Close period now' button) with no visual difference from a normal still-open period - no 'overdue' badge, no color change, no note that the automatic close didn't run. | The copy phrases the close date as if it's handled automatically ('every month on the 28th'), which reads as reassuring, but nothing on screen tells the owner that promise didn't happen for this period - possible cause is the daily Vercel Cron settlement auto-close job not firing in this environment rather than a code defect, but the UI gives no way to tell the difference. | When today's date is past nextAutoClose and the period status is still 'open', show a distinct overdue state (e.g. amber label 'Should have closed Aug 28' instead of the neutral 'Closes Aug 28' phrasing) so the owner can tell a stuck period from a normal one. (src/app/(app)/shared/period-card.tsx:75)
- [P3] 'Outstanding now: $42.11' chip; 'Clear split' link | Measured with the audit ruler: the 'Outstanding now: $42.11' text renders at ~4.07:1 contrast against its pink chip background (WCAG AA text needs 4.5:1). The 'Clear split' text link inside the row's split-metadata strip measures 38x44px, just under the 44px minimum tap-target width. 78% of all text characters on the page render under 16px. | Minor legibility/tap-precision polish; doesn't block the task but is measurable and easy to fix. | Darken the amount text (or lighten the chip background) on the Outstanding chip to clear 4.5:1; pad 'Clear split' to at least 44px wide.
- [P3] 'Transactions' stat-tile label | The stat tile's 'Transactions' label truncates to 'TRANSACTIO…' - the label needs 86px but the tile only gives it 84px, a 2px overflow. | Small polish nit that's avoidable with a couple points of width or a shorter label. | Widen the tile slightly or shorten the label (e.g. 'Txns') so it doesn't clip by 2px.
- [P3] Row-level Share toggle; Running Tally headline sentence | Two patterns worth the rest of the app copying: (1) the transaction row exposes Splits / Edit / Share / Always share / Delete inline with no swipe or overflow menu, and tapping Share commits instantly with an optimistic badge flip + toast, no dialog; (2) the /shared page's headline is a single plain-language sentence - 'Audit Partner owes Playwright $42.11' - genuinely sayable out loud, unlike the per-transaction joint-account line in J9c-F2. | This is exactly the zero-friction, no-review-step interaction the product model calls for, and the clearest 'who owes whom' phrasing seen across this and prior journeys (compare J9b-F2's silent, laggy Categorize-apply). | No change needed here - use this row-actions layout and this headline-sentence pattern as the template when touching Categorize-apply lag (J9b-F2) and the joint-account split wording (J9c-F2).

Could not test: Whether the daily Vercel Cron settlement auto-close job actually runs on schedule in real production - this local production-build test shows a period 1 day past its stated close date still open, but that may be because the cron simply doesn't run against this dev/E2E environment rather than a product defect. Would need Vercel Cron logs on the real deployment to confirm. | 'Detect e-Transfers' button on /shared - not opened; would need bank rows labelled as e-Transfers to test meaningfully and I didn't want to create data outside the journey's ask. | 'Record a payment by hand' form - located its fields (From/To member selects, Amount pre-filled with $42.11, Date, Note) via the DOM but did not submit it, to avoid altering the settlement tally outside the journey's scope. | 'Close period now' - not tapped; closing the open period is a one-way action not requested by this journey and would affect the shared state for any later journeys. | Whether the fixes/shared-page.tsx / fixes/shared-row.tsx redesign pack (found in the repo, not requested by this task) is already applied to what's live at localhost:3000 or still pending - the on-screen page matches the pack's 'after' description, suggesting it's applied, but I didn't diff the files to confirm.

### J10 - Day 14, owner - "Partner paid me back - settle up and close the period."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 10, keystrokes 0, scrolls 4, decisions 0, route changes 2, return taps 1.
Key numbers: outstanding_before_payment_dollars = 42.11; outstanding_after_payment = All square (0); record_payment_fields_total = 5; record_payment_fields_prefilled_correctly = 4; record_payment_fields_optional_left_blank = 1; record_payment_stuck_saving_min_seconds_observed = 10; record_payment_toast_shown = false; record_payment_inline_success_message_shown = false; record_payment_server_action_result = ok:true (200), confirmed only after a forced full-page reload; close_period_confirm_names_consequence = true; mark_not_shared_confirm_names_consequence = true; delete_payment_confirm_names_consequence = true; share_all_unflagged_has_confirmation = false; share_all_unflagged_is_destructive = false; settlements_route_redirects_to = /shared; period_status_after_close = Settled (auto, since 0 outstanding at close); period_overdue_days_at_close = 1; page_scroll_height_px = 3775; viewport_height_px = 844; screens_of_scroll_to_reach_payment_form = 4; small_tap_target_found = Clear split button, 38x44px (pre-existing, noted in J9c).

I landed on /shared and immediately saw the thing I came for: 'Audit Partner owes Playwright $42.11' with an 'Outstanding now: $42.11' headline.
I scrolled to the bottom and found 'Record a payment by hand' - tapping it expanded a form that was already filled in for me: From=Audit Partner, To=Playwright, Amount=$42.11 with an 'Outstanding: $42.11' hint right under it, and Date=today.
Only the optional Note was blank.
Genuinely nothing to type - I just had to confirm.
That's exactly the kind of low-friction moment this app should be built around, and I want to call it out as the pattern the rest of the app should copy.
Then I tapped Record payment and the button flipped to a disabled 'Saving…' and just sat there.
I waited, checked the console, checked the network tab - a POST to /shared had already come back 200 OK - but the button never recovered, no toast fired, and the page's own 'Outstanding now' figure stayed frozen at $42.11 the whole time.
After ten-plus seconds of nothing I gave up and force-reloaded the page.
Only then did I see 'All square' - the payment had actually gone through on the very first tap.
The UI just never told me.
That's a real problem: I did the right thing once and the app made me think it hadn't worked.
With the debt cleared I went back up to 'Close period now', read a clear confirmation ('Everything shared up to today goes into a statement... the usual day-28 close is skipped this month'), cancelled it to check the wording, then did it for real.
It auto-marked the period 'Settled' since we were already square - no extra step.
Period history now shows 'Aug 1, 2026 - Aug 29, 2026 · Settled · Closed by Playwright · 1 payment · $0.00 settled', with the $42.11 payment line and a 'Delete payment' action next to it.
I also tested the two other confirmations the brief asked about: 'Mark every transaction as not shared?' ('Nothing is deleted') and 'Delete this payment?' ('Transactions involved aren't affected') - both cancelled cleanly, both name exactly what's at stake.
'Share all unflagged', a purely additive action, fires with no confirmation at all, which is correct. /settlements redirected straight to /shared, no dead end.
The one big blemish on an otherwise well-designed flow is that stuck Saving state - it's the kind of bug that makes a person distrust every other 'it worked' in the app.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Record payment submit button (bottom of page, 'Record a payment by hand' form) | Tapped Record payment with every required field already correct (Audit Partner -> Playwright, $42.11, today). The button switched to a disabled 'Saving…' and stayed there for 10+ seconds with no toast and no inline confirmation, even though a POST to /shared had already come back 200 OK in the network log. The page's own 'Outstanding now: $42.11' headline never updated either. I only found out the payment had actually gone through - it had, on the very first tap - by force-reloading the page and seeing 'All square'. | Settling up is the entire point of this screen. A payment that silently succeeds server-side but looks hung/failed client-side, with no toast and no visible success state, forces the user to abandon the page and reload just to learn whether their money was recorded. On a real device with no devtools to check, a person would either sit staring at a frozen 'Saving…' not knowing whether to wait, force-quit, or worry the payment vanished - none of which match what actually happened (it worked instantly). | In src/app/(app)/shared/record-form.tsx the form binds React's useActionState directly to <form action={formAction}> (lines 30-34); every other action on this page (Close period at period-card.tsx, Mark as not shared / Share all unflagged at bulk-actions.tsx) instead uses useTransition + startTransition and correctly returns to an idle state after the server responds. Reproduce with devtools open, submit the form, and trace why `pending` never flips back to false despite the action resolving with {ok:true} at actions.ts:296; the existing success message ('Payment recorded', record-form.tsx:107-111) is fully coded but never gets the chance to render. Add a client-side timeout/fallback so a pending state that hasn't cleared within ~5s surfaces an error/retry affordance instead of hanging forever. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/record-form.tsx:30, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/record-form.tsx:113) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J10\03-record-payment-stuck-saving.png)
- [P2] 'Record a payment by hand' section placement | The only form for manually settling up sits at the very bottom of the page - past all 17 transaction share-rows and the empty period-history/shared-per-period teasers - while 'Close period now' sits right at the top under Running tally. Reaching the payment form took roughly 4 screens of scrolling (page is 3775px tall on an 844px viewport). | Settling up and closing the period are the two halves of the same routine job this screen exists for (its own tagline is 'Split fairly. Settle quickly.'), yet the two actions are placed about as far apart as the page allows, adding avoidable scrolling to the most common reason to open /shared. | Move RecordSettlementForm (rendered at page.tsx:505) up next to OpenPeriodCard (rendered at page.tsx:356, contains the 'Close period now' button), or add a 'Settle up' shortcut inside the Running tally card that scrolls to / expands the existing bottom form, so both settle-up actions are reachable from the top of the page. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/page.tsx:356, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/page.tsx:505)
- [P3] Record-a-payment-by-hand form fields | Of the form's 5 fields (From, To, Amount, Date, Note), 4 were correctly pre-filled with zero typing: From = the member who owes, To = the member owed, Amount = the exact live outstanding balance with an 'Outstanding: $42.11' hint underneath, Date = today. Only the optional Note was left blank. | This is exactly the 'just confirm and tap' pattern the product model calls for, and it stands in sharp contrast to friction found elsewhere in this app (e.g. the cost-split editor from an earlier journey needed hunting through a 17-option account dropdown to find a transaction again). Worth calling out explicitly as the pattern other manual-entry forms in the app should copy. | No change needed here - flagging as a positive pattern. When building other manual/fallback entry forms (e.g. a manual account balance, a one-off transaction), default every field the app can compute from context the same way this form does, and leave only genuinely optional fields blank. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/record-form.tsx:35) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J10\02-record-payment-form-prefilled.png)
- [P3] 'Outstanding now' headline vs 'Total shared this account' stat | After settling, the page header correctly reads 'All square', but a stat further down still shows 'Total shared: $42.11 · this account' and the Loblaws row still shows 'Audit Partner owes $42.11' in its per-transaction split line. Both are correct in isolation (one tracks the running debt, the other tracks how much of this account's spend is flagged shared, unaffected by payment), but at a glance the page shows two different '$42.11' figures with opposite implications after the debt is actually cleared. | A user scanning quickly right after settling up could reasonably wonder whether the payment really cleared the balance, since a prominent $42.11 is still visible on the same screen. | Consider labeling the 'Total shared' stat more explicitly as a period total independent of settlement status (e.g. 'Total shared this month' rather than sitting directly under a now-resolved 'All square' banner), or visually de-emphasize it relative to the Running Tally card once a period is fully settled. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/page.tsx:423)
- [P3] Confirmation dialog wording (Close period, Mark as not shared, Delete payment) | All three confirmation dialogs tested name exactly what happens and what doesn't: closing says shared expenses go into a statement and the usual day-28 close is skipped this month; marking-as-not-shared says 'Nothing is deleted'; deleting a payment says 'Transactions involved aren't affected'. Meanwhile the one clearly non-destructive bulk action, 'Share all unflagged', fires immediately with no confirmation at all (verified in bulk-actions.tsx). | This is the right split - confirm only what's destructive or hard to undo, and when confirming, state the actual consequence rather than a generic 'Are you sure?'. Worth calling out as the pattern to keep using everywhere else in the app that has destructive actions. | No change needed - positive pattern to preserve. The shared ConfirmButton component (src/components/ui/confirm-button.tsx) with its `prompt`/`description` props is doing this correctly; keep using it for future destructive actions instead of a bare window.confirm() or an unconfirmed delete. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shared/bulk-actions.tsx:34) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J10\06-mark-all-not-shared-confirm.png)

Could not test: Whether the stuck 'Saving…' state on Record payment is deterministic or intermittent - only one submission was tested. Repeat attempts across fresh sessions, ideally with an uncaught-exception listener, would confirm whether it fails every time. | Whether a repeating console error present since initial page load ('RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded' in 'restoreNativeMatchMedia' / 'tryLater', not found anywhere in the app's own source via grep) is a contributing cause of the stuck Saving state or just coincidental noise from the shared browser session/extension environment - would need a clean browser profile to rule out. | What a non-owner household member sees for Close period / Delete payment / Mark as not shared - this journey only covered the owner (Playwright); permissions for these actions from a partner's account were not tested. | What 'Detect e-Transfers' opens - out of scope for this brief, not exercised.

I opened /budgets to see what went over this month.
August's hero card immediately says '$5,908.38 over' in a red pill, and one row - Audit Coffee - is over its own budget too.
I tapped back to July to see what the month-switcher does: the header cleanly reads 'July 2026' with < > arrows, and a 'This month' link appears (only when I'm not on the current month) to jump straight back - a good, unambiguous pattern.
Standing budgets (Audit Food $400, Audit Coffee $50) carried into July automatically since they're 'Every month', and July correctly shows $0 spent since no transactions exist there yet.
Back on August, I edited Audit Coffee to 70.
The field arrives pre-filled with '50.00' and the caret sits at position 0, not selected - typing '70' produced '7050.00', the exact same concatenation bug I found on Day 1 (J2-F2), still unfixed 15 days later.
I cleared it properly and saved 70.00; no toast fired, just a quiet re-render, but the number stuck.
I toggled the 'Every month / August only' pill to see what it does: a title tooltip (not on-screen text) explains 'Every month' is the standing amount and 'August only' pins a one-time override for just this month without touching the standing value - useful, but you'd never learn this without long-pressing for the tooltip.
I toggled it back to Every month and moved on.
Then I tried to move $50 from Audit Food to Audit Coffee.
There is no move-money control anywhere - I had to compute 400-50=350 myself, type it into Food, then compute 70+50=120 and type it into Coffee, then hit one shared Save for both.
Neither field's 'Over by' text nor the hero total recalculated live while I typed - I had to trust my own arithmetic until Save resolved.
After saving, I found something worse: the hero card's total dropped to 'of $350.00' - LOWER than before - even though Food+Coffee together now total $470.
Digging into the code confirmed why: the headline total only sums top-level categories' own budget amounts, silently excluding any subcategory's budget (like Coffee's), while the numerator does roll children's spend into the parent.
So budgeting at the subcategory level - which the app itself encourages, since Coffee has its own row, its own progress bar, its own toggle - makes the household's real total invisible and inflates every 'over' figure.
Moving to /pnl, the monthly bar strip and 'Top categories' list read the period clearly ('August 2026' with the same < > pattern) and the GST split from J4 landed exactly where it should (Taxes: $25.00, matching Audit GST).
But the 12 month-bars only show a two-digit month number on screen - the actual income/expense numbers exist only in an aria-label, invisible to a sighted touch user; reading any single month means tapping through a full page reload.
Income itself gets one lump number with zero source breakdown, unlike the rich expense-side category list right below it. /net-worth was the most interesting page: at rest it's just a flat green line with no axis and no visible numbers besides 'Today'.
But I discovered - simply by tapping/holding on the chart - that it scrubs beautifully: a dotted guide-line and marker appear, and the headline swaps to that exact month's net worth plus assets/liabilities, no navigation needed.
This is genuinely the best 'decision-useful' widget in the whole journey - it's just completely undiscoverable, since cursor:crosshair (its only interactivity hint) doesn't exist on a touchscreen.
I also noticed the chart's first 20 of 24 months are a dead-flat $100,645.78 (the account-creation opening-balance backfill), drawn with the exact same solid stroke as the three real, dramatic months that follow - which could read as two years of real stability when it's really three months of data and a backfilled flatline.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] 'This month' hero card total ('$X of $Y' and 'N of 13 categories budgeted') | After moving $50 from Audit Food to Audit Coffee (Food 400->350, Coffee 70->120), the hero total flipped from 'of $400.00' to 'of $350.00' - lower than before - even though the household's combined budgeted amount across the two categories actually rose to $470. 'N of 13 categories budgeted' stayed at '1 of 13' the entire session, never counting Audit Coffee despite it having its own amount field, its own progress bar, and its own 'Over by' text. | totalBudget is computed by filtering budgetByCat to only categories with no parent (page.tsx:144-146), summing just the parent's own entered amount - a subcategory's budget is invisible to the headline total. But totalActual is computed from actualRolled (page.tsx:147-149), which DOES roll a child's spend up into its parent. So the numerator counts child spend while the denominator drops child budgets, silently inflating every 'over' figure for any household that budgets at the subcategory level - which the UI itself invites by giving Coffee its own full row. | Sum budgetByCat over ALL categories (matching the rolled logic already used for totalActual) for the headline total, and count a parent+child pair as 'budgeted' in the 'N of 13' line whenever either has a nonzero amount. - [P1] per-category 'Budgeted amount' textboxes + single shared 'Save budgets' button | There is no move/transfer control anywhere on the page. Moving $50 from Audit Food to Audit Coffee required opening Food's field, mentally computing 400-50=350 and typing it, then separately opening Coffee's field, computing 70+50=120 and typing it, then tapping one shared Save. Nothing links the two edits as a transfer, and neither field's 'Over by' text nor the hero total recalculated live while typing - the Coffee row's 'Over by $18.87' text was still showing the pre-edit figure the instant after I typed '120.00' into the amount field next to it. | Rebalancing category budgets at month-end is a named, explicit use case for this feature, yet the only way to do it is to invent a two-step manual-arithmetic workaround and trust it blind until Save resolves - with no preview of the resulting numbers before committing. | Add a 'Move $ to another category' action (e.g. from a kebab menu on the row, or a button next to 'Over by') that opens a small transfer sheet: from-category pre-filled, to-category picker, amount - applying both deltas atomically with a live preview of both new totals before Save. Typing '70' character-by-character (as a real phone keyboard would) produced '7050.00', not '70.00' - the exact same concatenation bug documented in J2-F2 on Day 1, unfixed 15 days later. | The input has no onFocus select() handler, so every pre-filled numeric field is a landmine: a distracted user who doesn't scroll down to double-check the result could save a wildly wrong budget with zero warning or validation. | Select the entire existing value on focus (e.g. onFocus={(e) => e.target.select()}) so typing replaces it, matching standard mobile numeric-field behavior. Reading one month's numbers requires tapping the bar and waiting through a full route change/reload (~3s), then repeating per month to compare. | Touch has no hover, and this chart provides no in-place value reveal at all - unlike the sibling net-worth trend chart, which lets a tap/drag preview any point's exact numbers instantly, in place, with no navigation. | Make bar taps preview inline (a small popover/label above the tapped bar showing that month's income/expense) with the current tap-to-navigate as a secondary action, or add always-visible value labels the way the 'Top categories' list below already does. Most users will only ever see the endpoint value and the small 'Building a year of history…' caption. | Add a persistent, lightweight visual hint - e.g. a faint vertical guide at the endpoint at rest, a one-line caption 'Drag to see any month' under the chart, or a few small month tick labels along the bottom edge - so the interaction is discoverable without accidentally touching the chart. The P&L can't show an income breakdown because there's nowhere for income to be categorized distinctly in the first place. | Add a symmetric 'Income sources' list (grouped by category or payee) below the expense breakdown, and/or add an Income-type category group so payroll/interest/refunds have somewhere the P&L can report on. This flat stretch is the accounts' opening-balance backfill, not real financial history, but it is drawn with the identical solid green stroke as the real portion. | The only hint this isn't real is 'Building a year of history…', a small gray caption below the headline number, disconnected from the graphic. A user skimming the chart's shape - especially paired with the page's own heading 'Two years of patience.' - could easily read 20 flat months as genuine stability rather than a backfill artifact. | Visually distinguish the backfilled segment before earliestRealMonth (already computed server-side) - e.g. a dashed or lighter stroke, or simply clip the chart to only the months with real data until enough history accumulates.
