### J12 - Day 16, owner - "Are we on track for the emergency fund, and what is the car loan costing?"

Goal reached: NO. Taps 24, keystrokes 62, scrolls 1, decisions 8, route changes 6, return taps 0.
Key numbers: goals_add_button_click_attempts = 5; goals_add_button_dom_change_detected = false; goals_active_count_before_and_after = 0; loans_save_terms_hang_ms = >38000 (POST /loans returned 200 within the first few seconds per network log); loans_rate_change_add_ms = <3000; loans_household_debt_total_owing = 176775.89; loans_household_projected_debt_free = August 2030 (after adding the 7.25% rate change; was July 2030 before); loans_household_remaining_interest = 2757.17; loans_car_loan_next_payment_split = $349.95 principal + $100.05 interest (before rate change); contributions_rrsp_available_before = 66810; contributions_rrsp_contributed_before_and_after = 0; contributions_rrsp_input_covered_by_tabbar_at_scroll0 = true; time_off_concatenation_bug_repro = pre-filled '0.00', caret index 0 on focus, typed '8' -> field value '80.00'; time_off_vacation_balance_after_save = 8.00 h; balance_sheet_linked_accounts_shown = 9; balance_sheet_page_itself_has_manual_entry = false; balance_sheet_update_balances_dialog_editable_rows_total = 11; balance_sheet_update_balances_dialog_editable_rows_plaid_linked = 9; balance_sheet_update_balances_table_width_px = 560; balance_sheet_update_balances_wrapper_width_px = 341; balance_sheet_assets = 212441.42; balance_sheet_liabilities = 186427.66; balance_sheet_net_worth = 26013.76; net_worth_hero_today = 26013.76; net_worth_hero_matches_balance_sheet = true; net_worth_hero_built_from = live Plaid account balances, confirmed by exact match to /balance-sheet's Assets-minus-Liabilities for the same month; keyboardFit_loans_rate_vs_save_kb336 = {"input_y":312,"submit_y":599,"submit_bottom":643,"keyboard_top":508,"submit_covered":true}; keyboardFit_contributions_rrsp_vs_save_kb336 = {"input_y":650,"submit_y":983,"submit_bottom":1027,"keyboard_top":508,"submit_covered":true}; keyboardFit_timeoff_accrued_vs_save_kb336 = {"input_y":473,"submit_y":739,"submit_bottom":785,"keyboard_top":508,"submit_covered":true}; toast_count_this_session = 0.

I open the More sheet from the dashboard and tap Goals, expecting a quick way to set an emergency-fund target - this is literally the app's stated purpose for this screen ('Add a target and watch the leaf bar climb').
The page is empty and the only affordance is a green 'Add goal' button.
I tap it.
Nothing happens - no sheet, no navigation, no error, no console warning.
I try again from a fresh page load with a clean Playwright click, then with a raw DOM .click(), then by invoking the React fiber's onClick handler directly.
Every method 'succeeds' with zero exceptions, yet the DOM never changes by a single byte and no dialog ever mounts.
This is a dead primary CTA on the one screen I was sent here to use, and it fully blocks both 'Audit Emergency fund' and 'Audit Old Goal' from ever being created - I cannot mark progress on goals that don't exist.
I move on to Loans, which works well: I set 6.49% / Jan 2023 / $25,000 original / $450 monthly on Audit Car Loan.
Save terms hangs on a disabled 'Saving...' for 38+ seconds with zero feedback even though the network tab shows the POST returned 200 almost immediately - only a reload reveals the amortisation schedule (payoff July 2030, $2,480.27 remaining interest).
Adding a rate change (7.25% effective Jan 2027) is the opposite experience: it saves in under 3 seconds, shows 'Rate change saved.', and the amortisation table recomputes live and correctly.
Remove -> 'Remove this rate change?' -> Cancel works exactly as it should.
Contributions is billed as 'current-year contributions' but the only editable RRSP field is 'Room this year' (an allowance override, literally labelled 'your Notice of Assessment figure') - there is no field to log an actual contribution.
'Contributed' is derived entirely from transactions inside an account typed 'rrsp', and this household has none (the linked Plaid RRSP account was mis-mapped to 'Taxable investment' during import, per an earlier journey), so RRSP Contributed will read $0.00 forever regardless of what I type here.
The RRSP room input itself sits at y=799-843 on first paint, directly under the fixed tab bar, so a first-attempt tap there hits the nav instead of the field.
Time off reproduces the exact same 'pre-filled 0.00, caret lands at index 0' bug documented on the Budgets screen weeks ago: typing '8' into the Accrued field produces '80.00', not '8.00'.
Balance sheet is where the real news is: the page itself is exactly right - every account, including all nine Plaid-linked ones, shows a live, correct, un-editable balance with no manual entry anywhere.
But the 'Update balances' button opens a dialog titled 'Balances - August 2026' with copy 'Enter each account's balance as of August 2026' and a text input, pre-filled with the current Plaid balance, for literally every account - Plaid and manual alike, no distinction, no badge, no 'synced automatically' notice.
It invites the owner to hand-retype nine live bank balances every month, and the table itself doesn't even fit the dialog (560px table in a 341px wrapper), so reaching the input requires horizontal scrolling inside a vertically-scrolling modal.
Net worth redeems the story: the $26,013.76 hero for August 2026 matches the balance sheet's Assets ($212,441.42) minus Liabilities ($186,427.66) exactly - it is genuinely built from live Plaid data, not anything typed.
I leave the household with real loan terms and a rate history on Audit Car Loan, a (functionally inert) RRSP room override, and 8 vacation hours logged - but with zero goals, because the button to make one does not work.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Add goal button (green primary CTA in the empty-state card, and in goal-controls.tsx's Sheet trigger) | Tapping 'Add goal' does absolutely nothing - no sheet opens, no navigation, no error, no console warning, no DOM change of any kind. | Reproduced with 5 independent methods on a fresh page load: a normal Playwright role-based click, a coordinate click, a raw DOM .click() on the exact button node, dispatched pointerdown/mousedown/pointerup/mouseup/click events, and a direct call to the button's own React onClick prop pulled from its fiber (which executed with no thrown exception). None of the five changed document.body.innerHTML by a single byte or opened MAPLE_AUDIT.dialogs(). The source (goal-controls.tsx) shows a completely ordinary useState(false)+onClick={() => setOpen(true)}+<Sheet open={open}> pattern that should work; something in this production build/hydration is silently breaking it specifically for this component, since an unrelated button ('More', which uses a different sheet implementation) on the exact same page opens correctly. | Fix whatever prevents GoalControls' setOpen(true) from taking effect in the production bundle (check for a hydration mismatch, a stale/mismatched RSC client-reference manifest entry for goal-controls.tsx, or a build-cache staleness issue specific to this chunk). Until fixed, this is a full outage of the Goals feature - the empty state, and the identical PageHeader-variant button used once goals exist, share the same broken component, so no user can ever create a first goal, and existing users likely cannot add a second one either. (src/app/(app)/goals/goal-controls.tsx:14, src/app/(app)/goals/goal-controls.tsx:39) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J12\02-goals-after-click.png)
- [P1] 'Update balances' button and its 'Balances · <Month>' dialog (form.tsx) | The button opens a dialog titled 'Balances · August 2026' with the copy 'Enter each account's balance as of August 2026' and a text input for every single account in the household - all 9 Plaid-linked accounts (Plaid RRSP, Plaid Mortgage, Plaid Student Loan, etc.) included, each pre-filled with its current Plaid balance as an editable, savable value, with zero visual distinction from the two manual accounts. | This directly contradicts the app's own stated model that a Plaid user never wants to type or maintain a balance. The dialog doesn't merely tolerate manual entry for unsupported accounts - it actively invites re-typing the live balance of every linked bank/loan/investment account every month, with the placeholder value pre-populated from the very Plaid feed it would then be shadowing. The underlying feature (a locked-in month-end snapshot, distinct from the live 'current' balance) is legitimate for point-in-time reporting, but the UI gives no indication that Plaid accounts don't need this, no 'synced automatically' badge, and no way to exclude linked accounts from the form. | For any account with a live Plaid balance, replace the editable input in this row with a read-only 'Live: $X (synced from bank)' display and drop it from the form's submittable fields entirely - or at minimum add a clear per-row 'synced' badge and move Plaid rows to a separate, collapsed, non-editable section, so the only editable inputs left are the household's genuinely manual accounts (Audit Cash, Audit Old Account, and similar). Typing '8' the way a real phone keyboard would (character by character) produced '80.00', not '8.00'. | Identical root cause to the long-standing budgets-page bug (a bare <input defaultValue={n.toFixed(2)}> with no onFocus select-all and no numeric masking) now confirmed on a fourth distinct screen (previously seen on Budgets amounts, Contributions rooms share the same pattern, and now Time off hours). This is a systemic, app-wide input-component gap, not a one-off. | Build one shared MoneyInput/DecimalInput component that selects its entire value on focus (or clears a default '0.00' on first keystroke) and reuse it everywhere a pre-filled numeric field appears - Time off, Budgets, Contributions, Loans - instead of each screen hand-rolling its own <input defaultValue=...>. (src/app/(app)/time-off/table.tsx:41, src/app/(app)/time-off/table.tsx:108) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J12\09-time-off-filled.png)
- [P2] 'Save terms' button on the loan-terms form (loans/form.tsx submit) | After tapping Save terms, the button flips to a disabled 'Saving…' and stays there for 38+ seconds of active watching, with no spinner, no toast, no progress indicator. The underlying POST /loans request actually completed with a 200 well within the first few seconds (confirmed in the network log), but the client-side UI never reflects it - only a full page reload reveals the terms saved correctly. | This is the same class of hung-save bug documented repeatedly in earlier journeys (category adds, budget edits, transaction adds, rules) - a revalidatePath fan-out or client transition that doesn't settle even though the server-side mutation succeeded almost instantly. On a phone, a user has no way to distinguish this from a genuinely broken save and will very plausibly re-tap, navigate away thinking it failed, or wait far longer than necessary. | Resolve the pending/Saving… client state directly from the server action's own return value/promise resolution rather than relying on Next's revalidation+re-render cycle to settle it - the fix should make the button return to its resting state within roughly 1-2 seconds of the actual server response, matching what the 'Add' rate-change control on this same page already does correctly. 'Contributed' is instead computed entirely from transactions inside accounts explicitly typed 'rrsp' - and this household has zero such accounts (the linked 'Plaid RRSP' account was mis-mapped to type 'Taxable investment' during Plaid import, a bug already flagged in an earlier journey), so RRSP Contributed will read $0.00 forever, no matter what is typed into this screen. | A user asking 'are we on track for retirement' has no way to see or record a real RRSP contribution here at all - the screen looks actionable (progress bar, 'Contributed $0.00 of $66,810.00') but is structurally inert for this household until the account-type mapping bug is fixed elsewhere. | Either (a) surface a visible warning on this page when a member has $0 Contributed AND zero accounts of that registered type ('No RRSP account linked - link or tag one to track contributions automatically'), or (b) fix the upstream Plaid account-type mapping so 'Plaid RRSP' is actually typed rrsp, which would make this page work as designed with zero further UI change. I started cold on the dashboard and needed to find the CSV import tool for my bank that Plaid can't reach.
There's no import link on the dashboard itself, so I went to Transactions, tapped the small 'More actions' overflow button, and found two options in a sheet: 'Set up sync' (which I already know from a prior day is actually a Gmail-alert importer, not the real bank sync) and 'Import CSV'.
I tapped that and landed on a genuinely well-built page: a paste box up top, then two supporting cards pitching 'Hands-free' Gmail auto-import and 'Connect a bank' via Plaid, then a plain-language list of what columns it looks for.
I pasted my six-row CSV and the page reacted instantly and correctly - no submit button needed to see a preview: it auto-detected Date/Description/Amount, showed '6 rows detected', and rendered a live per-row preview with dates, account, and category, one of which (Tim Hortons) it had already smart-suggested as Audit Coffee.
I changed 'Default account' from the pre-selected 'Audit Car Loan' (alphabetically first, same bad default I've seen everywhere else in this app) to 'Audit Old Account' and watched every preview row update its Account field live.
I tapped 'Import 6 transactions' and got a plain inline confirmation, 'Imported 6 transactions.' - no toast, consistent with the rest of the app.
Then I pasted the identical CSV again to test duplicate handling, and this is where the wizard shines: it recomputed the preview to '0 new · 6 match existing' and, per row, printed 'Enriches an existing transaction from 2026-08-20 - no duplicate.' I tapped the resulting 'Import 0 new · enrich 6' button and got 'Imported 0 transactions, enriched 6 existing.' No accidental duplicates, clear language about what happened - genuinely the safest re-import behavior I've seen in this app.
Then I went to check whether the imported rows joined the same uncategorized pile as bank-synced transactions, and they did - the pill went from '6 need a category or title' to '11', meaning 5 of my 6 new rows (all but the auto-suggested Tim Hortons) sit in the exact same queue as Plaid rows.
Good, that's the product model working as intended.
But then I actually read the individual rows and found something bad: Costco, which I entered as -184.22 (an outflow), shows in the transaction list as '+$184.22' - a credit.
Petro-Canada, also entered negative, shows '+$62.40'.
Amazon shows '+$15.00'.
And the one row that actually was money coming IN - 'E-TRANSFER RECEIVED,250.00' - shows as '-$250.00', an outflow.
Every single imported row has its sign flipped relative to what I pasted and relative to what the wizard's own preview showed me right before I hit Import.
I checked the code and found the exact bug: the 'Auto (respect minus sign)' branch computes the stored sign backwards from the field's own documented convention.
This is a real, silent, whole-CSV data corruption bug on the exact scenario this journey exists to test.
Finally I opened the auto-setup page as a first-time reader without configuring anything.
It's a 6635px, ~8750-character page: generate a webhook secret, go change alert settings inside my actual bank's website, open script.google.com in a separate tab, paste ~100 lines of JavaScript into a Google Apps Script project, set a script property, deploy it as a Web App, copy an /exec URL back into Maple, then build per-bank routing rules.
The code block itself clips its longest lines with no visible horizontal-scroll cue.
There is no way I could do this on my phone in the moment; I'd need a laptop, several open browser tabs, and real technical comfort - a total contradiction of the page's own teaser copy ('Hands-free... no credentials, no monthly fees') sitting right on the CSV import page I'd just used.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Default sign convention ('Auto (respect minus sign)') applied to a pasted CSV | Pasted a 6-row CSV with standard bank-export sign convention (debits negative, e.g. '-184.22' for a Costco purchase; the one credit, an e-transfer, positive '250.00'). The wizard's own live preview showed the correct signs before import ('-$184.22' for Costco, '$250.00' for the e-transfer). After tapping Import, every single row's direction flipped in /transactions: Costco shows '+$184.22' (an inflow), Petro-Canada shows '+$62.40', Amazon shows '+$15.00', and the one genuine inflow, E-TRANSFER RECEIVED, shows '-$250.00' (an outflow). | This is the exact scenario the product exists to support - a statement from a bank Plaid can't reach - and it silently corrupts every row's money direction while claiming success ('Imported 6 transactions.'). A household relying on this path to keep their numbers straight now has a wrong outflow/inflow split and, unless they manually check every row against their real statement, a wrong picture of their spending baked into the same uncategorized pile as their bank-synced rows. | In wizard.tsx, the field comment on line 84 documents the invariant: 'amountCents: number // signed: positive = outflow'. The 'auto' branch (lines 255-256) currently does `signed = wasNegative ? -Math.abs(amountAbs) : Math.abs(amountAbs)` - a CSV row written as negative (a debit/outflow) ends up with NEGATIVE amountCents, which by the app's own convention means inflow. Flip it to `signed = wasNegative ? Math.abs(amountAbs) : -Math.abs(amountAbs)` (matching the sign the manual-direction branch on line 258 already uses correctly), and add a regression test that pastes a negative-amount CSV row and asserts the resulting transaction renders as an outflow with a minus sign after import, not just that amountCents is negative. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/import/wizard.tsx:84, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/import/wizard.tsx:255) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J13\07-transactions-sign-flip-bug.png)
- [P1] The whole 5-step 'auto-import' setup flow, teased as 'Hands-free' on the parent /transactions/import page | The CSV import page's top card reads 'Hands-free / Want every purchase to land here automatically? Forward your bank's transaction-alert emails through Gmail... no credentials, no monthly fees.' Following it as a first-time reader lands on a 6635px-tall page (about 8750 characters of text, 98% of it under 16px) that actually requires: generating a webhook secret in Maple, going into your bank's own website to turn on $0.01 transaction alerts, opening script.google.com in a separate Google product, pasting ~100 lines of JavaScript into a new Apps Script project, setting a script property, deploying it as a Web App, copying the resulting /exec URL back into Maple, building per-bank email-parsing rules, then sending a test email through the pipeline. The Code.gs block itself is a fixed monospace box whose long lines (e.g. the WEBHOOK_URL constant) are clipped at the right edge with no visible scroll affordance. | This directly contradicts the product's own stated model that manual setup is the rare exception and the user never wants to type or maintain anything - here the 'automatic' path is actually a self-maintained integration across three different products (Maple, a bank's own settings, Google Apps Script/Gmail) that a phone user cannot complete in the moment, while being marketed with copy ('no credentials, no monthly fees, hands-free') that implies the opposite of what the page actually demands. | Either relabel the teaser card on /transactions/import - drop 'Hands-free' and 'no credentials, no monthly fees' and replace with something like 'Advanced: Gmail + Apps Script (technical, ~15 min, do this on a computer)' so expectations match reality before anyone taps into it, or add a lightweight 'email me this setup link' action at the very top of the auto-setup page itself so a phone user isn't expected to paste code and juggle multiple tabs on-device. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/import/page.tsx:54, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/transactions/import/page.tsx:58) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J13\09-autosetup-code-block.png)
- [P3] Merchant description cleanup on import | The wizard rewrites raw bank text into cleaner merchant names on save: 'COSTCO WHOLESALE #123' became 'Costco', 'TIM HORTONS 4521' became 'Tim Hortons' - both good. But 'SQ *7734 TORONTO ON' (a Square terminal purchase) became just '7734 Toronto', dropping the 'SQ' marker that signals it was a card-present Square sale and keeping an opaque terminal ID instead, which is less legible than the original, not more. | Minor, but this is exactly the kind of cryptic-description problem the app is otherwise trying to solve (per J3's findings on raw bank text left untouched) - here the cleanup pass actively makes one row worse rather than leaving it alone. | In the merchant-name cleanup step, when a description starts with a known processor prefix like 'SQ *', either strip the whole processor+ID token (leaving just 'Toronto ON' or nothing to clean, falling back to the raw text) rather than keeping the numeric ID, or special-case 'SQ *' the same way other prefixes are presumably already handled.

Could not test: Whether the sign flip is a true storage-level inversion (amount_cents stored backwards) versus purely how the transaction list resolves that value to a label - not confirmed by a direct DB/network payload read; code inspection of wizard.tsx's 'auto' branch strongly points to a genuine storage-level inversion, and the Accounts page couldn't help disambiguate since Audit Old Account is a manual (non-Plaid) account whose balance tile shows a fixed 'Opening' value rather than a transaction-derived running balance. | Whether the non-default sign conventions ('All rows are outflows' / 'All rows are inflows') have the same inversion - only the default 'Auto' path was tested, since that's what a real user pasting a debit-negative bank statement (the placeholder example the tool itself shows) would leave selected. | Whether an OFX/QFX file import (same page, advertised 'Sign convention auto') hits the same buggy code path - the brief specified CSV only. | Whether the Gmail Apps Script auto-import flow actually works end-to-end (Send test email, Sync now, per-bank rule presets) - the brief explicitly said to read only, not configure, so Generate secret / Add a rule / Send test email were not exercised.

### J14 - Day 18, owner - REPORTED BUG - "The bank stopped syncing and nothing told me."

Goal reached: NO. Taps 12, keystrokes 9, scrolls 1, decisions 1, route changes 11, return taps 0.

Your accounts and their existing transactions stay - they just won't update automatically.; disconnect_confirm_buttons = ["Cancel","Disconnect"]; disconnect_confirmed = false; taps_dashboard_to_reauthenticate_button = 2; scrolls_dashboard_to_reauthenticate_button = 1; route_changes_dashboard_to_reauthenticate_button = 2; pull_to_refresh_label_sequence = ["Pull to sync","Release to sync","Syncing…","Pull to sync"]; pull_to_refresh_result_text_visible_on_page = none; sync_now_attempts_total = 3; sync_now_success_count = 0; reload_count_post_reauth = 2; reload_result_unchanged_count = 2; notification_types_available = ["New transaction","Large transactions only","A category goes over budget","A bank alert couldn't be imported","Shared expenses closed - time to settle"]; notification_type_for_bank_login_required = none; cron_can_retry_login_required_items = false; status_active_set_only_by = ITEM/LOGIN_REPAIRED Plaid webhook (src/app/api/plaid/webhook/route.ts:140-141).

I ran the reset script first: it force-put the Tartan-Dominion item into ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED in Plaid sandbox and printed status_before='active'.
Then I cold-opened /dashboard exactly like a real morning check.
Nothing.
Net worth card, income/spent/saved, six account tiles including all my Plaid ones, 'Where it went' - not one word about the bank.
I went to /accounts: eighteen rows, the fourteen Plaid ones sitting there with their usual '$0.00 Opening' label bug from an earlier day, but no badge, no icon, nothing marking them as broken. /transactions: an '11 need a category or title' pill, which is about categorization, not sync health - still nothing about the bank itself.
So per the product's own model, an interrupted feed should be surfaced, and right now it is invisible everywhere a normal person actually looks.
I pulled to refresh on /transactions (MAPLE_AUDIT.pull()): 'Release to sync' -> 'Syncing...' -> back to 'Pull to sync', no result text, no error toast, nothing different on screen.
Only when I went to /transactions/import/plaid-setup did the truth show up: a 'Re-auth needed' pill on Tartan-Dominion Bank of Canada, and a Recent-syncs row timestamped from that exact pull, reading 'Re-auth needed · +0 new · 0 enriched'.
So the pull DID detect the break, it just told nobody except this one settings sub-page.
I tapped Sync now there just to see what would happen before fixing anything: it did NOT run a sync - it popped 'No connected banks to sync.', which is flatly false, my connected bank is sitting right there in the list two lines above the message.
From a fresh /dashboard I counted the path to Re-authenticate: tap Settings (1), scroll ~700px to find the Bank connections card (1 scroll), tap 'Manage bank sync ->' (2nd tap) - that's 2 taps and 1 scroll to reach the button, landing on the same plaid-setup page.
Before pressing Re-authenticate I opened Disconnect to read it (never confirming): 'Disconnect Tartan-Dominion Bank of Canada?
Stops syncing and removes the secure connection.
Your accounts and their existing transactions stay - they just won't update automatically.' Cancel, good, nothing touched.
Then I tapped Re-authenticate.
The same update-mode Plaid Link iframe opened, skipped straight to a password screen for Tartan-Dominion (no bank list, no username re-entry - it remembered the institution and username), I typed pass_good, submitted, got the 'Save with Plaid' upsell screen, and tapped Finish without saving.
Maple showed 'Re-authenticated.
Syncing...' - a clear success message.
But the pill next to Tartan-Dominion, still on screen the whole time, never changed from 'Re-auth needed'.
I waited five more seconds: still 'Re-auth needed'.
I tapped Sync now again: 'No connected banks to sync.' - same false message as before re-authenticating at all.
I did a hard reload: still 'Re-auth needed', Recent syncs still shows only the original failed pull, no new entry at all despite two Sync-now taps and one full reauth.
I tried Sync now once more (still blocked, same message) and reloaded a second time - identical result.
I went back to /setup: the Bank connections card still reads 'One bank needs attention.' Back to /dashboard: still zero mention of any of this, even now that the bank has been broken, 'repaired', and still broken again for several minutes.
I checked the code to understand why, since this looked like more than a demo quirk. triggerPlaidSync's own query only selects items with status IN ('active','error') - login_required is deliberately excluded, which is exactly why Sync now says 'no connected banks' the moment a bank needs re-auth.
And the only place that ever flips status back to 'active' is the ITEM/LOGIN_REPAIRED Plaid webhook handler - nothing else touches it, including the daily cron sweep, which itself only ever looks at status='active' rows. usePlaidReauth's onSuccess calls triggerPlaidSync(id) immediately after Link closes and unconditionally shows 'Re-authenticated.
Syncing...' regardless of what that call returns - so even in production, that call races the webhook and, if the webhook hasn't landed yet (or never reaches a non-public host, as here), the reauth flow lies to the user about having fixed anything.
I also checked the push-notification code: there are exactly three notification types (new transaction, budget overspend, unmatched email alert) - none for a bank losing its connection, which is the single most silent, most consequential failure mode this app has.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] entire cold-open surface (no element - an absence) | After forcing the Tartan-Dominion item into ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED, /dashboard, /accounts, and /transactions show zero indication anything is wrong at any point in the journey - not before any sync attempt, not after a failed pull-to-refresh sync, and (per J14-F3) not even after a failed re-authenticate. The 14 Plaid account rows on /accounts render identically to healthy ones; the /transactions pill only ever counts uncategorized rows, never sync health. | Confirmed programmatically: a full-page text scan of /dashboard for 'reauth'/'re-auth'/'attention'/'needs' returned false even at the very end of the journey, after the bank had been broken, 'repaired', and silently still broken for several minutes. A user who doesn't know to visit Settings -> Bank connections -> Manage bank sync has literally no way to discover their feed stopped - their balances and 'Where it went' totals just quietly go stale forever. | Surface bank health on the pages people actually look at: a dismissible banner on /dashboard ('Tartan-Dominion needs attention - reconnect') when any plaid_items row for the household has status in (login_required, error, revoked), and a badge on the affected account rows on /accounts and inside the account-tile flip-card on /dashboard, not just on a settings sub-page three taps deep. (src/app/(app)/setup/bank-connections.tsx:28) (screenshot: 01-dashboard-before-sync.png)
- [P1] Sync now button (per-bank row) | Tapping Sync now on a bank whose pill reads 'Re-auth needed' - with that exact bank visibly listed one line above the button - pops the alert 'No connected banks to sync.' This happened identically before re-authenticating and twice more after, on every single Sync-now tap in the journey. | triggerPlaidSync's own item query filters `.in('status', ['active', 'error'])`, which deliberately excludes login_required items. The function then falls through to `items.length === 0` and returns the generic 'no connected banks' error - the exact same message a household with zero banks linked at all would see - even though a bank IS connected, it just needs re-auth. The message actively misleads the user about what's wrong. | When the requested item(s) exist but are excluded only because of status, return a distinct message ('This bank needs re-authentication before it can sync - tap Re-authenticate.') instead of reusing the generic 'No connected banks to sync.' string, and disable/relabel the Sync now button itself (e.g. grey it out, or replace its label with 'Re-authenticate') for any row whose status is login_required so the dead-end isn't discoverable only by tapping it. - [P1] Re-authenticate button / update-mode Plaid Link flow | Completed the full, correct re-auth: tapped Re-authenticate, went through Plaid's update-mode Link with user_good/pass_good, tapped Finish without saving. Two more manual Sync-now taps (both still 'No connected banks to sync.') and two hard reloads later, the item is still 'Re-auth needed', the Recent-syncs log has gained zero new rows since the original failed pull, and /setup still reads 'One bank needs attention.' There is no way, from inside the app, to get this bank syncing again. | usePlaidReauth's onSuccess calls `triggerPlaidSync(id)` immediately when Plaid's Link onSuccess fires, and unconditionally sets the notice to 'Re-authenticated. Syncing...' regardless of what that call returns. But the app's own plaid_items.status column is only ever flipped back to 'active' by the ITEM/LOGIN_REPAIRED Plaid webhook handler - nothing in the reauth success path updates it directly. Nothing else retries it either: the daily cron sweep only ever queries status='active' rows, so a login_required item can never be picked up by the safety net. The user did everything the app asked and is left permanently stuck with no error explaining why. | Don't show 'Re-authenticated. Syncing...' until the reauth's own outcome is confirmed - either await a server round-trip that flips status to active (a dedicated endpoint the Link onSuccess calls directly, not a race against a webhook), or have triggerPlaidSync accept login_required items immediately after a successful update-mode Link exchange and clear the status itself instead of relying solely on the webhook. Also have the cron sweep (or a dedicated retry job) periodically re-check login_required items so a webhook that never arrives isn't a permanent dead end. ### J15 - Day 19, owner, in public - "Hide the numbers while I check a balance, then find last week's coffee."

Goal reached: NO. Taps 11, keystrokes 2, scrolls 0, decisions 2, route changes 7, return taps 1.
Key numbers: hide_balances_persists_across_nav = false; hide_balances_persists_across_reload = false; hide_balances_available_on_accounts_page = false; hide_balances_is_true_mask_or_css_blur_only = css-blur-only (DOM text unchanged, confirmed via MAPLE_AUDIT.money()); accounts_row_balance_label = OPENING; accounts_row_audit_cash_shown = $123,456.78; dashboard_card_audit_cash_live = $125,889.68; accounts_vs_dashboard_balance_gap = 2432.9; accounts_row_audit_visa_shown = -$4,321.00; balance_sheet_audit_visa_live = $2,967.02; search_SQ_results_in_current_month = 0; search_Toronto_results_in_current_month = 1; search_transaction_actual_title = 7734 Toronto; search_is_live_filter_as_you_type = false; search_requires_enter_to_submit = true; search_scoped_to_selected_month_only = true; search_all_time_option_exists = false; filter_sheet_account_options_count = 19; filter_sheet_category_options_count = 17; filter_show_results_worked = true; filter_chip_remove_worked_within_session = false; filter_chip_clear_all_worked_within_session = false; filter_eventually_cleared_after_seconds = true; filter_clear_apparent_delay_taps = 3; networth_page_horizontal_overflow_px = 27; networth_sr_only_table_actual_box_px = 377x624 (should be ~1x1); networth_docScrollWidth = 417; networth_innerWidth = 390; balance_sheet_horizontal_overflow = false; balance_sheet_truncation_count = 0; accounts_truncation_count = 0; dashboard_truncation_count = 0; money_signs_dashboard = {"none":70,"minus":6,"plus":6}; money_negativeToneNoSign_dashboard = 6; toast_count_this_session = 0.

I open the dashboard already signed in and see the Net worth hero, tiles, and account cards - real dollar figures, in plain text, in what the brief calls a public setting.
I tap the eye icon labeled 'Hide balances.' It flips to 'Show balances' and every dollar figure on the card blurs - a nice touch visually.
But I want to check one balance and then browse, so I navigate to /accounts.
The moment I land there the numbers are completely readable again - no hide toggle exists on this screen at all, and the state I just set never travelled with me.
I go back to /dashboard expecting my hide choice to have stuck.
It has not: the button reads 'Hide balances' again, plain numbers on screen, as if I had never tapped it.
So the one privacy control in this app only survives as long as I do not touch anything else, which defeats the point of having it in a public place.
I also notice the 'hide' effect is a CSS blur - the real numbers are still sitting in the page text underneath, unchanged.
On /accounts I try to answer 'which number is the balance' for Audit Cash.
The row shows $123,456.78 under a small grey 'OPENING' label.
That is not what is in the account today - the dashboard card for the same account read $125,889.68 a minute earlier.
The row is quietly showing the balance from the day the account was opened, not today's balance, and nothing about the label warns me it is stale.
Next, transactions.
I tap search and get a proper search keyboard - good.
I type 'SQ,' expecting to find a coffee charge that showed 'SQ *7734 Toronto' on my statement.
Nothing filters as I type.
I press Enter and the page reloads to '0 transactions - no transactions in August.' Confused, I try 'Toronto' instead, and there it is - one row, titled just '7734 Toronto.' The SQ prefix was silently stripped when the row was imported, so my search for the exact text on my statement fails; the app already cleaned away the very fragment I remembered, and search only looks at the current month besides.
I open the Filter sheet, pick an account, and 'Show results' works fine - a chip with a count and an inline remove button, a good pattern.
But tapping the chip's 'Remove' button and the row-level 'Clear' button both do nothing, for several taps and many seconds.
The filter only actually clears later, seemingly by coincidence, when I reopen the Filters sheet for an unrelated reason.
For a while I genuinely believed the controls were dead.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] Hide balances toggle (eye icon, top-right of dashboard hero) | Tapping 'Hide balances' blurs every figure on /dashboard. Navigating to /accounts and back to /dashboard - or simply reloading - resets it to fully visible, and no hide control exists anywhere outside the dashboard (not on /accounts, /transactions, /net-worth, or /balance-sheet). | The control is plain component-local React state (useState(false) in dashboard/client.tsx) with no localStorage, cookie, or server persistence, and it is not wired to any other screen. Someone who taps 'Hide balances' specifically to glance at their phone in public loses that protection the instant they tap anything else, including the one screen (Accounts) most likely to be checked next. | Persist the hidden/shown flag in localStorage (or a user preference row) and read it on every screen that renders money, including /accounts, /transactions, /net-worth, and /balance-sheet, not just /dashboard. Ship the same eye toggle in the header/shell so it is reachable everywhere. (src/app/(app)/dashboard/client.tsx:147, src/app/(app)/dashboard/client.tsx:238) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots/J15/02-dashboard-after-hide.png)
- [P1] Account row balance figure, labeled 'OPENING' | Every account row on /accounts shows its balance under a small grey 'OPENING' label - and that figure is frozen at the value typed/synced the day the account was created, not today's balance. Audit Cash reads $123,456.78 here while the dashboard card, /balance-sheet, and /net-worth all correctly show $125,889.68 (a $2,432.90 gap) for the exact same account. | The Accounts screen is the single most natural place to answer 'what is my balance,' and this journey's own goal is checking a balance. The row renders account.opening_balance_cents verbatim with no live figure anywhere on the row, so a user glancing here for a real answer is silently shown a number that can be off by thousands of dollars, with a label that does not say 'stale' or 'as of account creation.' | Replace the row's displayed figure with the account's live current balance (the same value already computed correctly for the dashboard cards and balance-sheet), and relabel it 'Balance' (or 'Balance today'). If the opening balance is still needed for reference, move it into the Edit sheet, not the primary list row. (src/app/(app)/accounts/row.tsx:172, src/app/(app)/accounts/row.tsx:179) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots/J15/04-accounts-rows-visa-cash.png)
- [P1] Search box, and the stored transaction description for CSV-imported rows | Searching 'SQ' - the literal 'SQ *' prefix that appears on real bank/card statements for Square-processed purchases - returns 0 results in every month, even though the matching transaction exists. Searching 'Toronto' (part of the cleaned title) finds it instantly: the row's actual title is '7734 Toronto', with 'SQ *' silently stripped at import time and the original raw text discarded, never stored anywhere. | cleanTitle() strips leading processor noise (SQ *, TST*, PYPL *, SP*, POS, etc.) and the import path writes the cleaned string directly into the searchable description column, overwriting the raw statement text permanently. This defeats the exact 'find last week's coffee' scenario the whole app exists for whenever a user's memory anchors on the very fragment the app just deleted - a realistic case for any Square/Toast/PayPal/Shopify-processed purchase, which is common at cafes, food trucks, and small retailers. | Keep the raw imported description in a separate column (e.g. raw_description) alongside the cleaned display title, and have the search query ILIKE against both columns (or a concatenation of the two) so a user can find a row by either the clean name or the original statement text. The filter only actually cleared later, apparently as a side effect of reopening the Filters sheet for an unrelated tap. | Both controls call the same clearAll()/navigate() function, which wraps router.replace() in a useTransition. The logic itself reads correctly (empty string clears each query param), so the failure is a client-side responsiveness/staleness bug, not broken logic - but a real user sees an unresponsive control with zero loading feedback and no way to tell 'processing' from 'broken', which is exactly the kind of server-tap friction the product explicitly should never impose. | Show a visible pending/disabled state on the Clear and per-filter-remove buttons the instant they are tapped (the useTransition's isPending flag is already available and unused), and investigate why router.replace() is not landing promptly on this route - likely the same revalidation-latency pattern already logged in J8 (six-fold revalidatePath fan-out) rather than a new bug. - [P2] Search box + month navigator | Search is always scoped to whichever month is currently in view (the ilike is combined with a gte/lt occurred_on range for that month), with no all-time or date-range option. Searching 'SQ'/'Toronto' from August found nothing until I manually paged back to check July, and the actual match turned out to still be in August - I had no way to search the whole ledger at once. | A user trying to find a specific past purchase rarely remembers the exact month, especially for a one-off charge; having to page month-by-month while re-running the same search term is slow and easy to give up on. | Add an 'All time' option next to the month nav (or auto-widen the search when a query is present and returns 0 results in the selected month, with a prompt like 'No matches in August - search all months?'). - [P2] Search box | Typing 'SQ' does not filter the visible list at all - the count stays '44 transactions' and the full unrelated list stays on screen until Enter is pressed, which triggers a full route navigation (?q=SQ) and reload rather than an in-place client-side filter. | A live-filter-as-you-type search feels instant and lets a user course-correct mid-word; this implementation commits to a full round trip before showing any result, which is slower on a real network and gives no feedback that typing is even being recognized as search input. | Either filter the already-loaded month's transactions client-side as the user types (for the common case of searching within the current month) or debounce a client-side re-fetch, reserving Enter/route-navigation only for changing months or when the list is not fully loaded. - [P3] Hidden-balance rendering (PrivacyBlur component) | MAPLE_AUDIT.money() returned byte-identical dollar text before and after tapping 'Hide balances' - the numbers are still present verbatim in the DOM/accessible text, only visually blurred with CSS. | A screen reader, in-page Ctrl+F search, or 'view source' still exposes every real figure even while the button claims balances are hidden, which is a smaller privacy gap than the primary shoulder-surfing case but still a mismatch between what the control promises and what it does. | When hidden, replace the rendered text (or set aria-hidden + a masked '••••' text node) rather than only applying a CSS blur filter over the real value, so assistive tech and text search also respect the hidden state. This table is supposed to be invisible to sighted users and is instead adding real, if usually unnoticed, horizontal scroll to a page whose primary content (the net worth chart) does not need it. | Fix the sr-only utility to actually clip to 1x1px for table elements (some `sr-only table` combinations lose the effect to table's default display rules) - e.g. add explicit width:1px;height:1px;overflow:hidden to this table, or move the caption/rows into a <caption class='sr-only'> + individually sr-only cells pattern instead of hiding the whole <table>. I started on the dashboard, already signed in, and went hunting for the housekeeping chores one at a time.
Renaming a category on /categories was painless: I tapped Edit on Audit Transfers, the Name field came pre-filled and focused, I retyped it, tapped Save, and the row updated instantly with no reload needed - one of the few writes today that felt trustworthy.
Archiving that same page's categories was just as clean: I created Audit Old Cat (though the Add button hung on 'Adding...' for 10+ seconds before I had to reload to see it landed), then tapped Archive, read a genuinely good confirmation ('hidden from budgets and the transaction picker, existing transactions keep their category, unarchive anytime'), confirmed, and watched it move into a 'Show archived (1)' bucket immediately.
Then I went to /accounts to archive Audit Old Account and hit real friction: the confirm dialog was just as clear, but after I confirmed, the Archive button went permanently disabled with no 'Archiving...' label, the row never left the active list, and the 'Archived (0)' counter never moved - for the entire time I sat watching.
Only a full page reload revealed the archive had actually succeeded on the server all along.
Same shared ConfirmButton component as categories, wildly different reliability.
Audit Old Goal I couldn't even attempt - Add goal on /goals does absolutely nothing.
No dialog, no network call I could see change anything, not even when I called the onClick handler directly from the console.
This is the exact same dead button J12 found 20 days ago, still dead.
On /rules I found both confirmations excellent: 'Apply to last 12 months' explained exactly what it would and wouldn't touch, and Delete named the precise number of transactions ('unshare the 6 transactions this rule shared') with hand-edited splits called out as protected - genuinely the best confirmation copy in the app.
I cancelled both. /setup's household rename and notification prefs were unremarkable in a good way - simple inline edit, prefs correctly remembered from days ago.
No dark-mode control exists anywhere, confirmed by a full sweep of the More sheet.
Sign-in was where I found a real annoyance: typing a wrong password wiped BOTH the email and password fields, not just the password, forcing a full retype; the error itself, 'Invalid login credentials,' is raw Supabase text, out of step with the app's otherwise friendly copy.
On a 336px keyboard, the Sign in button sits under it, though Enter still submits so it isn't blocking.
Forgot-password, the unconfirmed-email redirect to check-email with a working Resend, and the invalid-invite-token page were all clean, well-copywritten dead ends.
I finished back on the dashboard as the owner, greeting intact.

Findings on the way:

The row stays in the active list and the 'Archived (0) →' link at the top of the page never updates. I watched for 8+ seconds with zero visible change. Only a full page reload showed 'Archived (1)' and the row correctly moved out of the active list - the archive had actually succeeded on the server the entire time. | This is the same class of stale-UI-after-mutation bug flagged repeatedly since Day 1 (J2, J7, J8, J12), now confirmed on account archiving specifically. What makes it worse here is the total absence of any pending-state label - a user has no way to tell 'still working' from 'silently failed' from 'button is just broken', and might re-navigate away or retry elsewhere, unaware the action already worked. | Give the ConfirmButton trigger a visible pending label (e.g. 'Archiving…') the same way SubmitButton does elsewhere, and investigate why revalidatePath('/accounts') + revalidatePath('/dashboard') from archiveAccount() does not flow through to a re-render within several seconds on this route, when the same ConfirmButton + revalidateCategoryConsumers() pattern on /categories (which revalidates six paths) updates the list instantly. Try again or reset your password.' in the signIn() action before returning it as AuthState.error. (budgeting-web-app/src/app/(auth)/actions.ts:61)

(budgeting-web-app/src/app/(auth)/sign-in/page.tsx:77)

Deleting a rule opens a dialog that says exactly what will and won't change: 'New transactions will no longer match this rule,' plus a checked-by-default checkbox, 'Also unshare the 6 transactions this rule shared - Splits you edited by hand are never touched.' It names the precise count affected and explicitly protects hand-edited data. | Compare this to weaker confirms elsewhere in the app that don't name a count or an edge-case protection (e.g. the 'Mark every transaction as not shared?' dialog documented in J10, which describes the mechanism but not how many rows or which protections apply). | Reuse this exact pattern - named affected-row count + an opt-out checkbox that explains what's protected - on every other destructive confirm in the app that touches a variable number of rows (mark-not-shared, category archive when transactions exist, account archive, etc.). ### J17 - Any day, owner - "Use it as an installed app on iPhone."

Goal reached: yes. Taps 6, keystrokes 0, scrolls 1, decisions 0, route changes 16, return taps 0.

I set out to check whether Maple actually behaves like an installed iPhone app, not just a mobile-friendly website.
I flipped the browser into an iPhone 17.5 Safari user agent and, since Chromium's raw CDP display-mode emulation turned out not to take effect here even after re-applying it and reloading three times, I patched matchMedia/navigator.standalone at the JS level to genuinely exercise the app's own standalone-detection branch.
With that in place, /dashboard, /transactions, /budgets and /setup all rendered pixel-identical to plain browser mode - same header, same fixed tab bar at y=785, no extra chrome to worry about - and, more importantly, the 'Install Maple to your home screen' hint correctly disappeared, confirming the app's own matchMedia/navigator.standalone check works.
Flipping back to genuine Safari mode (dismissedAt cleared) brought the hint straight back, sitting as a floating pill that visibly covers the bottom half of the Income/Spent/Saved tiles until you dismiss it - dismissing it worked cleanly and stuck across a reload, respecting a sane 14-day TTL I found in the code.
Touch worked well once I routed around a Playwright limitation (touchscreen.tap refused to fire because the context lacks hasTouch, so I dispatched raw CDP touch events instead): a tap on the Budgets tab navigated correctly, a tap on a transaction's Categorize button opened the Quick-category panel, and a real touch drag on the dashboard cycled through 'Pull to sync' -> 'Release to sync' -> back to 'Pull to sync' exactly as documented in earlier journeys.
Offline is where the story splits in two.
Staying on an already-loaded dashboard and going offline is handled gracefully: a calm 'Offline - showing what was last loaded' banner appears, and the Add Transaction sheet still opens fine since it's pure client-side UI.
But tapping ANY tab bar link while offline forces a fresh navigation, and that hits the service worker's deliberate network-first policy for authed pages - it lands on a completely bare offline.html with just an 'M' logo, 'You're offline,' and a Try again button.
No tab bar, no way back to anything, and the copy hard-codes 'read your transactions' even when the failed route was /dashboard.
Given the product's own philosophy of categorizing in place on the bus or wherever you are, this is a real dead end, not just a rough edge.
The manifest itself is solid - correct start_url, standalone display, portrait lock, and all four icon variants (192, 512-any, 512-maskable, apple-touch) resolve with 200s.
Lighthouse mobile audits came back excellent: 100/100/100/100 on /dashboard, and 96/100/100/100 on /transactions with the only miss being a real, code-confirmed contrast bug on the 'This month' pill.
Zero tap-target failures on either page.

Findings on the way:

- [P1] public/offline.html service-worker fallback page | Going offline and then tapping any tab (Transactions, Budgets, Home via a fresh nav) replaces the whole app with a bare 'You're offline' card and a single Try again button - no tab bar, no Home link, nothing. Even /dashboard, loaded successfully seconds earlier in the same session, hits this identical dead end on the next fresh navigation while offline. There is no way back into the app until connectivity returns; Try again just re-shows the same page while still offline. | The product's whole daily job is categorizing transactions wherever the user happens to be (explicitly 'on the bus', 'coffee in hand' in earlier journeys) - a real connectivity blip during that is common, and it fully strands the user with zero navigation options, unlike staying on an already-loaded page offline (which shows a calm banner and keeps working, see the same session's screenshot 09). | Give offline.html the same tab bar/nav the rest of the app uses (or at minimum a 'Back to Home' link), and/or have the service worker's networkFirstPage() fall back to a cached last-good shell for GET navigations to already-visited routes instead of the standalone static file - keeping the 'never cache live financial data' policy intact by re-fetching fresh data once online, exactly like the in-place offline banner already does for the dashboard. (budgeting-web-app/public/offline.html:84, budgeting-web-app/public/sw.js:149) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J17\07-offline-transactions-tab.png)
- [P2] IOSInstallHint floating status banner | On first paint (or any time the 14-day dismissal has expired), the 'Install Maple' pill floats in fixed position and visually covers the bottom half of the Income/Spent/Saved tiles - real numbers the user is trying to read - the moment the page loads, before any user action. | It obscures live financial data (part of the at-a-glance dashboard) with a promotional banner the user did not ask for and has not yet had a chance to dismiss. | Anchor the hint lower (e.g. only after the user has scrolled past the summary tiles once) or shrink/reflow it so it sits in the existing gap above the tab bar without overlapping the Income/Spent/Saved row - the same slot the offline banner and the toast already use cleanly. (budgeting-web-app/src/components/pwa/ios-install-hint.tsx:1, budgeting-web-app/src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:731) (screenshot: ux-audit/shots\J17\06-install-hint-safari-mode.png)
- [P3] Static 'This month' pill next to the month nav arrows | Lighthouse's mobile accessibility audit (96/100, single failure) flagged this exact element for insufficient contrast: 2.38:1 against a 4.5:1 requirement. | The pill's text (already the muted `text-ink-3` token) is additionally rendered at `opacity-50`, stacking two dimming steps and pushing genuinely-there copy below WCAG AA legibility. | Drop the `opacity-50` modifier on the static (non-link) 'This month' pill, or swap to a token that alone clears 4.5:1 at 12px - don't combine an already-muted color token with a second opacity reduction. (budgeting-web-app/src/components/ui/month-nav.tsx:58)
- [P3] offline.html body copy | The offline page's only sentence is 'Maple needs the network to read your transactions. Reconnect and try again.' - shown identically whether the failed navigation was to /transactions, /dashboard, /budgets, or /setup. | A user who lost signal tapping Budgets or Home sees a message specifically about 'your transactions', which doesn't match what they were actually trying to do. | Make the copy route-agnostic, e.g. 'Maple needs the network to load this page.' (budgeting-web-app/public/offline.html:88)
- [P3] Reconnection feedback after going back online | Restoring connectivity after the offline banner was showing produced no toast or confirmation of any kind - the banner just silently disappears once a background check succeeds. | Minor: banner-removal is itself a legible signal, but it's easy to miss if the user isn't looking at that exact spot, and it's inconsistent with how most other state changes in the app do get an explicit (if quiet) confirmation. | Optional: a brief 'Back online' toast (reusing the existing role=status toast pattern) when connectivity is restored, matching the same visual language as the offline banner it replaces.

Could not test: Real iOS notch/status-bar-band rendering: env(safe-area-inset-top/bottom) resolved to 0px throughout this Chromium/CDP session (no notch geometry available via raw Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride), so the code-level safe-area padding (shell.tsx:483,526,545,600,605,638,734) could not be visually confirmed against a real notched iPhone - would need a real device or Xcode Simulator. | True native display-mode:standalone: CDP Emulation.setEmulatedMedia had no observable effect in this Playwright/Chromium build even after 3 re-applications and reloads; standalone-mode behavior was instead verified by patching matchMedia/navigator.standalone at the JS level, which correctly exercises the app's own detection branch but is not literally the browser engine's native signal. | Web Push notification permission/delivery for the standalone-only 'Enable notifications' flow (documented in an earlier journey) was not re-tested here - out of scope for this journey's checklist. | Self-inflicted test-instrumentation residue: my own matchMedia-patching init scripts (needed to swap standalone/Safari mode on the single shared tab, since Playwright's addInitScript has no public removal API) left a non-fatal 'RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded' firing ~3 times per page load for the rest of this shared browser session. This is a tooling artifact, not a Maple bug - the stack trace names (restoreNativeMatchMedia, tryLater) don't exist in Maple's codebase - but any journey running after this one that checks console-error counts on this shared tab should discount these 3 errors. | Related to the above: navigator.maxTouchPoints could not be fully reset to 0 in this shared session (CDP Emulation.setTouchEmulationEnabled({enabled:false}) did not revert it here); it remains 5 for later journeys sharing this tab. Low practical impact since most app code doesn't branch on that exact value, but noting it for anyone debugging odd touch-related behavior in a later journey. | Emulation.setUserAgentOverride({ userAgent: '' }) did not clear the prior iPhone UA override as might be expected from the brief's literal reset recipe - had to pass an explicit desktop Chrome UA string to actually revert it. Worth knowing if a future journey's own reset step relies on the empty-string form.

## Part 2 - Screen by screen (spec format)

```
SCREEN: every (app) route (judged on /dashboard, /budgets, /transactions/import) (U00 app-shell)
USER GOAL(S): Navigate between the app's daily-use destinations one-thumb; Reach the uncategorized-transactions pile fast from a cold open (J3); Quick-add a transaction from anywhere, including screens without their own add flow (J8); Know whether the app is online/syncing and whether an action just worked; Customize the bottom tab bar to the destinations used most
ELEMENT COUNT: 7 (primary 1, secondary 6, destructive 0, above fold 7) [code-estimate: persistent shell chrome only (top bar back/wordmark + settings gear, bottom bar 3 slots + FAB + More), all fixed/sticky so always above the fold at 390x844; More sheet, slot-placement overlay, confirm modal and toasts are separate overlay states counted in sub_screens]
TAPS TO PRIMARY GOAL: 1 (scrolls 0) - Cold open lands on /dashboard (Home tab already active). Tapping the Transactions tab (1 tap, 0 scrolls, 0 decisions) reaches the uncategorized pile directly - the shell itself puts J3 within budget. J8 (FAB from /budgets) is the failure case: see U00:fab-no-target-navigates-away - the FAB does not open an in-place add sheet from /budgets, it navigates to /transactions?add=1, which is an extra, unwanted screen change against the product model's "categorize in place" goal.
HEURISTIC SCORE: 15/20 (H1=2 H2=1 H3=2 H4=1 H5=1 H6=2 H7=1 H8=1 H9=2 H10=2)

FINDINGS
[P1] Bottom tab bar center '+' quick-add button | Tapping the FAB on any screen other than /dashboard or /transactions (e.g. /budgets, per J8) navigates the user away to /transactions?add=1 instead of opening an add-transaction sheet in place, losing whatever context they were in. | Only src/app/(app)/dashboard/client.tsx and src/app/(app)/transactions/tx-controls.tsx call useQuickAddTarget to register a handler. Every other (app) route has none, so QuickAddProvider.trigger() (quick-add.tsx:40-43) falls through to router.push(QUICK_ADD_FALLBACK), and the FAB in shell.tsx:552-563 calls that trigger unconditionally. | Register a lightweight add-transaction quick-add handler on every (app) route (or make the fallback open the add-transaction sheet as an overlay on top of the current route instead of a full navigation), so the FAB never leaves the screen the user was on. (src/lib/quick-add.tsx:27, src/lib/quick-add.tsx:40) [code]
[P2] ConfirmModal's confirm button (used for every destructive confirm app-wide) | The confirm modal auto-focuses the Confirm/Delete button even when destructive=true, and a bare Enter keypress anywhere in the open modal fires onConfirm - one stray Enter (external keyboard, iPad, autofill) destroys data with no extra step. | autoFocus is applied unconditionally on the action button (confirm-button.tsx:252, styled destructive at :255-259) and the keydown handler treats Enter as confirm regardless of the destructive flag (confirm-button.tsx:174-177). | When destructive is true, autoFocus the Cancel button instead of Confirm and/or drop the Enter-to-confirm shortcut, so a destructive action always needs an explicit tap on Confirm. (src/components/ui/confirm-button.tsx:252, src/components/ui/confirm-button.tsx:174) [code]
[P3] Sheet primitive and the shell's More navigation sheet | Bottom sheets only close via the X button, a scrim tap, or Esc - there is no swipe-down-to-dismiss gesture, even though both sheets render a grabber bar that visually implies one. | sheet.tsx registers no drag/touch handler on the panel (only Esc via keydown at line ~62 and a scrim onClick at line 102); the shell's hand-rolled More sheet (shell.tsx:622-712) has the same gap despite the grabber pill at line 640-642. | Add a pointer-drag handler on the sheet header/grabber that tracks downward drag distance and calls onClose past a threshold, matching the affordance the grabber already implies. (src/components/ui/sheet.tsx:113, src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:640) [code]
[P3] Overlay/focus-trap logic (More sheet, Sheet primitive, ConfirmModal) | Three separate hand-rolled implementations of the same modal/sheet pattern (focus trap, Esc-to-close, scroll lock, restore focus) exist side by side, so behavior already drifts between them and any future fix (e.g. the swipe-dismiss above) has to be applied three times. | shell.tsx's useOverlay (lines 124-178) duplicates the FOCUSABLE selector and Tab-trap logic already implemented in sheet.tsx (lines 8-9, 60-96), and confirm-button.tsx (lines 12-13, 167-204) duplicates it a third time. | Rebuild the More sheet and ConfirmModal on top of the shared <Sheet> primitive (passing custom content/footer props) so overlay behavior is defined exactly once. (src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:124, src/components/ui/sheet.tsx:60) [code]
[P3] Top-right Settings gear icon button | A persistent icon-only settings button sits in the top-right corner of every mobile screen - the hardest spot to reach one-thumb at 390x844 - and duplicates a destination (Setup) already reachable through the tab bar's More sheet. | shell.tsx renders the gear `justify-self-end` in the header grid on every route (lines 510-516); Setup is already one of the DESTS (line 55) surfaced through More. | Drop the persistent top-right gear and rely on Setup living in the More sheet, so there's one reachable path instead of an always-present top-corner one. (src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:510) [code]

(src/app/(app)/shell.tsx:46) [code]
[P1] Whole dashboard body (net worth card, Income/Spent/Saved tiles, account rail, 'Where it went') | Cold-opening the dashboard with 19 transactions sitting uncategorized gives zero signal that anything needs attention: no count, no banner, no CTA. Confirmed programmatically - the page's own text contains no occurrence of 'uncategorized', 'review', or 'needs category'. | The brief's whole product model is that categorizing is 'the user's whole daily job' - but the one screen the user opens every day never says the job exists. A user has to already know to tap into Transactions to discover the pill. | Add a dashboard tile/banner (near the top, above 'Where it went') reading e.g. '19 transactions need a category' that deep-links straight to the first uncategorized row on Transactions, and keep it in sync with the same count the Transactions pill shows. [P1] Quick category chip / 'More…' full-select Apply / '+ New' Create & apply (inline row actions) | In 4 of 6 in-place categorization attempts (McDonald's chip, Amazon full-select, Touchstone full-select, Sent '+ New'), the panel closed immediately after the tap and the row reverted to showing 'Uncategorized' with the Categorize button back - indistinguishable from the tap having failed. Network inspection showed every one of those POSTs actually returned 200, and the true category only appeared in the row after a full page reload. | The core promise of this screen is 'tap a chip, see it categorized, move on.' When the client silently fails to reflect a successful save, a user has no way to tell success from failure without opening devtools - they'll very plausibly re-tap, second-guess the app, or give up on a row they actually already finished. | Show the applied category optimistically the instant a chip/Apply/Create & apply is tapped (useOptimistic or local state update keyed off the response), and add a router.refresh()/cache-tag invalidation on the client after the server action resolves so the row never has to wait for an unrelated reload to catch up. (src/app/(app)/transactions/quick-categorize.tsx:38, src/app/(app)/transactions/actions.ts:251) [seen live: J3]
[P1] Create rule button | Tapped Create rule on a fully-filled-out Uber rule (merchant, category Transportation, Household-ratio share, apply-to-past checked). The button flipped to a disabled 'Saving…' and stayed there for 58+ seconds with no toast, no error, and no new network request ever visible. Only by navigating to /rules directly did I learn the rule had in fact been created and had already applied itself to 9 past transactions, retroactively marking 6 of them as household-shared. | This is the exact 'set it once, stop re-categorizing this merchant forever' automation the product model exists to encourage, and its own save button hangs indefinitely with zero feedback - a real user would assume the app froze, likely force-closing the tab or re-submitting (risking duplicate rules) long before the change actually lands. | Fix the client so the rule-creation mutation's resolution actually closes the dialog and shows a confirmation ('Rule created - 9 matches found, 6 now shared'); add a client-side timeout/error state so a genuinely stuck request tells the user something went wrong instead of hanging forever on a disabled button. [seen live: J3]

Paging back to July 2026 shows 18 transactions with 15 (83%) still Uncategorized - a backlog that's completely invisible from the August view, the pill, or the dashboard. | If a user only ever glances at 'this month's' pill, a large and growing pile of older uncategorized transactions can sit forever with no signal it exists - directly undermining 'the user's whole daily job is to categorize them.' | Make the pill (and any future dashboard banner) reflect a household-wide uncategorized count, not just the visible month, with a secondary line like '+15 more from earlier months' that jumps straight to the oldest unresolved month. The function then falls through to `items.length === 0` and returns the generic 'no connected banks' error - the exact same message a household with zero banks linked at all would see - even though a bank IS connected, it just needs re-auth. The message actively misleads the user about what's wrong. | When the requested item(s) exist but are excluded only because of status, return a distinct message ('This bank needs re-authentication before it can sync - tap Re-authenticate.') instead of reusing the generic 'No connected banks to sync.' string, and disable/relabel the Sync now button itself (e.g. grey it out, or replace its label with 'Re-authenticate') for any row whose status is login_required so the dead-end isn't discoverable only by tapping it. But the app's own plaid_items.status column is only ever flipped back to 'active' by the ITEM/LOGIN_REPAIRED Plaid webhook handler - nothing in the reauth success path updates it directly. SCREEN: cross-cutting: /dashboard, /transactions, /budgets, /accounts (+ every ResponsiveAmount/StatTile/MoneyInput consumer) (U01 money-formatting)
USER GOAL(S): Glance at any amount and know instantly whether it is a balance, a budget, spending, income, or what is left (zero taps); Read direction (money in vs money out) without relying on colour; Type a dollar amount (split, budget, opening balance, edit) on the iOS decimal keypad and have it accepted without hunting for the format; See whether this month is over budget, and by exactly how much, from a cold open of /dashboard; Hide balances with one tap and have that stick
ELEMENT COUNT: 20 (primary 1, secondary 16, destructive 0, above fold 7) [code-estimate]
TAPS TO PRIMARY GOAL: 1 (scrolls 1) - Cold /dashboard: reading net worth, Income/Spent/Saved is 0 taps. 'Am I over budget and by how much' is NOT answered on the default dashboard (DEFAULT_LAYOUT at src/app/(app)/dashboard/client.tsx:48 omits 'budget-progress'); it costs 1 tap (Budgets tab) and ~1 scroll past the hero, or 4 taps via Edit dashboard > +Add Budget progress > Save. Typing an amount is 2-3 taps (open editor, focus field, Done) in MoneyInput; 3+ in the raw inputs because there is no enterKeyHint and validation only happens server-side.

```
SCREEN: dashboard:edit-sheet (inside U02)
USER GOAL(S): Choose which cards show; Reorder cards; Reset to default
ELEMENT COUNT: Bottom sheet (Sheet primitive). Per visible row: Move up, Move down, Hide (3 x 44px, flush). Per hidden row: '+ Add' pill. Footer: Reset to default (text), Cancel (secondary), Save (primary). Header X, scrim, Esc. With the default 5 visible and 5 hidden: 15 + 5 + 3 + 1 + scrim = 25 controls in one list; Hick's exceeded but the list is scannable. Draft discarded on Cancel/scrim without confirm (acceptable, non-destructive). Save = sheet-footer, feedback = sheet closes.
HEURISTIC NOTE: H3 control ok (Cancel + Reset), H2 jargon in blurbs, H5 destructive Hide adjacent to move-down, H1 no preview of the result until Save.
FINDINGS: U02:greeting-hide-removes-editor-entry, U02:editor-jargon-and-hide-adjacency, U02:sheet-no-swipe-dismiss (see parent block)
```

```
SCREEN: dashboard:add-sheet (inside U02)
USER GOAL(S): Record a cash/manual transaction quickly
ELEMENT COUNT: Bottom sheet with AddTransactionForm: Spent/Received segment (2), amount (text, inputMode decimal, required, aria-label, 26px serif), date (type=date, default today), account select (no default, first option), CategorySelect (default '- Uncategorized -'), description (optional), submit 'Add transaction' inside the scrolling body, header X, scrim. 6 inputs, no enterKeyHint, errors as a banner above submit via aria-live. Offline: toast + single retry on reconnect, form stays filled. Success: form reset + sheet closes, no toast, no undo.
HEURISTIC NOTE: H5 dismiss drops draft, H7 no last-used account, H1 no save feedback, H9 error banner not field-anchored; decimal keypad and 16px inputs are correct.

```

Bulk (apply to same merchant) only exists inside the Review sheet: pill (2) -> chip (3) -> 'Save & next' (4).

```
SCREEN: /transactions (inline panel under a saved row after tapping 'Splits') (U04 transactions:row:split-panel)
USER GOAL(S): Split out the 5% GST from a categorized purchase into a second category row; Split one transaction across two or more budget categories; Correct or remove an existing split and see the row reflect it
ELEMENT COUNT: 10 (primary 1, secondary 7, destructive 2, above fold 6) [code-estimate]
TAPS TO PRIMARY GOAL: 8 (scrolls 2) - Cold open /dashboard -> Transactions tab (1) -> tap 'Splits' on the row (2) -> tap row-1 amount, retype total minus GST (3, mental arithmetic) -> tap 'Add split' (4) -> tap row-2 category select and pick the tax category from the full list (5-6) -> tap 'Apply $x to last split' (7) -> scroll to and tap 'Save splits' (8). If the user instead types the GST into the new row first (the natural order), the 'Apply remainder to last split' shortcut wipes that row back to 0 (split-editor.tsx:166-172), so the shortest working path needs the arithmetic. 2 decisions (which category, how much is GST). Target for a daily task is <=3 taps.
HEURISTIC SCORE: 8/20 (H1=1 H2=1 H3=1 H4=1 H5=0 H6=1 H7=0 H8=1 H9=1 H10=1)

FINDINGS
[P1] 'Apply $x to last split' button (split-editor.tsx:164-177) | The natural GST order is: Add split, type 2.50 into the new row, then hit the shortcut to fix the first row. Because the new row is now the last row and the remaining is -2.50, the shortcut subtracts 2.50 from the row the user just typed, resetting it to 0.00. The user sees their GST amount vanish and the chip still unbalanced. | The shortcut always mutates copy[copy.length - 1] (split-editor.tsx:169-170). After 'Add split' (split-editor.tsx:154 appends) the last row is the freshly typed GST row, so the helper the comment at split-editor.tsx:19-21 promises ('without running mental arithmetic') only works if the user first edits the original row by hand, which is exactly the arithmetic it was meant to remove. | Apply the remainder to the row that was NOT most recently edited: track lastEditedKey in updateRow and target the first row whose key !== lastEditedKey (fallback: row 0). Relabel the button 'Put $x on <category or 'row 1'>'. Additionally render a per-row 'Fill remaining' text button (min-h 44px) inside each amount cell when that row is not the one just edited. (src/app/(app)/transactions/split-editor.tsx:169, src/app/(app)/transactions/split-editor.tsx:154) [code]

The user's stated daily job includes splitting GST, and Canadian GST is a fixed 5% (plus provincial variants), so a one-tap preset is cheap and removes the arithmetic and the category pick. | Add a 'Split out GST (5%)' pill next to 'Add split' (min-h 44px, text-[13px]). On tap: compute tax = round(total - total/1.05) (tax-included default; long-press or a second pill for tax-on-top), reduce row 1 by that amount, append a row with amount = tax and category = the household's configured tax category (add a households.tax_category_id setting; fall back to the category most often used on split rows, from the same query that builds topCategoryIds in page.tsx:74-77). SCREEN: U04:split-panel (inside U04)
USER GOAL(S): Split out GST into a tax category; Divide one purchase across categories; Remove a split row or collapse back to a single category
ELEMENT COUNT: Per row: category select (44px), $-prefixed MoneyInput (44px), Remove X (44x44, only when >1 row). Panel: Add split pill, conditional 'Apply remainder' pill, Save splits (h-11 primary). Entry: 'Splits' / 'Hide splits' text toggle in the row action bar. With 2 rows: 10 interactive elements, ~6 above the fold when the row is at the top of the viewport. code-estimate.
HEURISTIC NOTE: Inline panel, not a sheet: no scrim, swipe or Escape; dismiss is the row's own 'Hide splits' toggle which discards edits. Decimal keypad and enterKeyHint=done are correct; 16px inputs avoid zoom. Status is a colour-coded chip plus text, so not colour-only, but it lags the keyboard.
FINDINGS: U04:apply-remainder-wipes-typed-row, U04:no-gst-preset, U04:unbalanced-submit-roundtrip, U04:zero-row-silently-dropped, U04:remaining-stale-until-blur, U04:income-sign-mismatch, U04:mobile-row-stacks-three-lines, U04:no-cancel-in-panel, U04:panel-opens-offscreen, U04:new-row-no-category-shortcut, U04:progress-bar-uninformative, U04:small-control-text, U04:saved-message-stale-no-undo, U04:select-optgroup-duplicate-and-no-name (see parent block)
```

SCREEN: /dashboard (sheet), /transactions (sheet), /transactions?add=1 (fallback from every other route) (U05 U05-add-sheet)
USER GOAL(S): Record a cash purchase (amount, today, cash account, category) in under 10 seconds one-thumbed; Record money received in cash; Create a missing category while adding; Abandon or correct a half-typed entry without losing it by accident; Add from any main screen via the tab-bar + and land back where I was
ELEMENT COUNT: 10 (primary 1, secondary 9, destructive 0, above fold 10) [code-estimate]
TAPS TO PRIMARY GOAL: 4 (scrolls 0) - Cold /dashboard: tap tab-bar + (1) -> sheet opens with focus on the dialog panel, not the amount (sheet.tsx:51) -> tap amount (2) -> type on decimal pad (no return key) -> tap iOS Done / outside to reveal submit (3) -> tap Add transaction (4). Adding a category costs +3 (open native select, pick, Done) = 7. Account is whatever is first in the list, not last used. A shorter path exists: autofocus the amount and default the account to last used/cash, which makes the minimum path 3 taps. Estimated sheet content height ~560px, under the 88dvh cap, so no scroll with keyboard closed; with the iOS keyboard up (~300px) the bottom ~40% of the fixed sheet is covered.
HEURISTIC SCORE: 13/20 (H1=1 H2=2 H3=1 H4=1 H5=1 H6=1 H7=1 H8=2 H9=1 H10=2)
